# YURI Full-System Shaping Sweep — Detailed Scopes (2026-06-04)

> Build appendix: the verbose per-item agent scopes + verification + drift findings from the 14-agent full-system sweep. Companion to the master plan (yuri-master-build-plan-2026-06-04.md). Each item is loadable by its implementing agent. Item ids are area-local; reference as "<AREA>: <id>". Advisory until built + locally verified.


---

# AREA 1: Circuitry layer: Energy & Math (computeU/yuri-energy, energy-tick-core, breaker, weights/config, FSRS, trace, claim-cortex/ledger, proof-gate, sim/experiment/dashboard tooling)

**Top priority:** ENG-01 (Cox per-class evidence aging into evalStaleness) — it is the catalog's own Build-Next #3, it simultaneously RESURRECTS the dead zeta/staleness branch (the one term verified to never fire), lands the first energy weight that can stop being hand-tuned, and directly hardens the standing PROSE-NOT-OUTRUN-WIRING rule (council-text/happy-path evidence must age faster than runtime-verified). Ships as one pure math-kernel function + a per-class multiplier with an existing test harness, zero live-gate risk in phase 1.

## Improvements

### ENG-01 — Cox proportional-hazards per-class evidence aging (resurrect the dead zeta term)
`math-transfer · effort M · risk med · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** replace-hand-tuned-constant, read-lower-bound-not-point  ·  **transfers:** catalog card 18 (Cox Proportional Hazards → per-class evidence aging)
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-energy.mjs:312 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/math-kernel.mjs:63 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/energy-tick-core.mjs:189 · 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/math-theory-transfer-catalog-2026-06-03.md:54
- **why now:** zeta is the only contribution term verified to never fire in real work; this is the highest-confidence way to make the energy fn use its full term set AND the first de-hand-tuning beachhead.
- **what:** evalStaleness in yuri-energy.mjs uses a single flat confidenceDecay (one halfLife for ALL evidence), and the live tick stamps every evidence record age:0 so zeta NEVER fires in real work (verified dead branch). Replace the flat decay with a proportional-hazards multiplier keyed on an evidence sourceClass: effectiveDecayRate = baseRate·exp(β·covariates). Phase 1: hardcode 3 multipliers (runtime_trace/test=0.3 ages slow, fixture=1.0, advisory/council_text=3.0 ages fast) — a live-runtime MATCH stays fresh, council-text 'verified_fact' ages out fast, so the ζ·staleness term rises faster for the classes YURI already distrusts (PROSE-NOT-OUTRUN-WIRING made mechanical). Phase 2 (deferred, needs the labeler from ENG-07): fit β by partial likelihood over (verified t0 → falsified t1) pairs scraped from the energy-trace. CRITICAL prerequisite for ANY effect: also fix energy-tick-core.applyTransition so evidence carries a real sourceClass AND the age axis actually advances across ticks (today age:0 is frozen) — otherwise the hazard multiplier multiplies a zero and the term stays dead.
- **agent scope:** 1) Add hazardMultiplier(sourceClass) to math-kernel.mjs with the closed-set map {runtime_trace:0.3, test:0.3, fixture:1.0, schema:1.0, advisory:3.0, report:3.0, operator_note:0.5} defaulting unknown→1.0 (fail-safe, not fail-open — an unknown class should NOT age slower). 2) In evalStaleness, read item.sourceClass and apply the multiplier to the effective halfLife (halfLife/multiplier) before confidenceDecay. 3) In energy-tick-core.applyTransition, stamp each pushed evidence record with sourceClass ('fixture' on a mutating edit, 'runtime_trace' on a passing Bash) and make daysSinceUse derive from capturedAt vs nowIso instead of the frozen age:0 (the record already stores capturedAt:nowIso — compute age at toGateState time). 4) Keep the negEntropy/flat path as a regression canary: with all-multipliers=1.0 and a real age, output must equal the current flat decay to the digit. 5) Add a worked-example fixture with mixed-class evidence asserting a council-text-heavy state crosses the reject threshold at a YOUNGER age than a runtime-heavy state.
- **verify:** node --test on a new yuri-energy staleness test: assert (a) multiplier=1.0 + age>0 reproduces flat confidenceDecay exactly (canary), (b) council_text(3.0) evidence yields a strictly larger staleness contribution than runtime_trace(0.3) at the same age, (c) a real tickAndTrace WORK replay across >halfLife simulated days now shows staleness in componentContributions (the dead term lit). Cross-check 'terms NEVER fired' shrinks from [malformedForecast, staleness] to [malformedForecast].

### ENG-02 — Wire the cortex's L∞ max-severity (maxLadderInversion) veto into the enforcing gate
`new-mechanism · effort L · risk high · OWNER-GATED` · deps: 

- **patterns:** gate-on-identity-not-aggregate, read-lower-bound-not-point  ·  **transfers:** informs catalog card 25 (Fisher/Beta lower-bound over-claim test) and card 35 (VCG externality penalty) which both want this veto surface first
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/claim-cortex.mjs:589 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/claim-cortex.mjs:530 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-energy.mjs:509 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/claim-ledger.mjs:114 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/energy-breaker.mjs:98
- **why now:** This is the single documented fail-OPEN in the gate (banked as memory FB:DELTA-GATE-SEVERITY-LAUNDERING); the swap-immune machinery is already written and tested but disconnected — wiring it is the highest-security-leverage move in the layer.
- **what:** claim-cortex emits maxLadderInversion (cortexSnapshot return, claim-cortex.mjs:530) as documented groundwork for the KNOWN delta-gate severity-laundering hole: gateProposal is a DELTA gate that cannot catch an equal-magnitude over-claim swap (resolve one 5-rung honestly while smuggling a fresh 5-rung fabrication; convex sum and even L∞ are conserved under the swap that 3²+4²=5² Pythagorean-partitions). The cortex already built the swap-immune answer: gateClaimTransition (claim-cortex.mjs:589) composes the delta gate with a per-claim IDENTITY veto (reject when ANY claim becomes a new-or-deeper RETRACT inversion, non-offsettable by resolving others) — but it has ZERO live consumer; the live ledger bridge (claimGateFields) deliberately OMITS promotionLadderInversions entirely, so theta is structurally present-but-always-0 in the live tick. This is the owner-gated step the cortex header and the DELTA-GATE-SEVERITY-LAUNDERING memory both name. Add an L∞ max-severity term inside gateProposal (or OR gateClaimTransition's identityVeto into the breaker's catastrophic class) so a severity swap can no longer pass.
- **agent scope:** Owner-gated (touches the enforcing energy core + the protected gate semantics). Path A (preferred, lower blast radius): keep gateProposal untouched; in claim-ledger.mjs add an optional second projection that, when an enforce flag is set, runs gateClaimTransition(before,after) and surfaces its identityVeto as a NEW catastrophic class to energy-breaker.isCatastrophic (alongside protectedPathVeto/structuralFloorVeto). Path B: add an L∞ term maxLadderInversionDelta inside gateProposal that vetoes when the per-claim max inversion depth rises above threshold, mirroring the structural-floor veto. Either way: the term must be NON-offsettable (no soft credit buys it back), must inherit the claimedRank≤RANK_RESEARCH hypothesis exemption so EXPLORE divergence is never punished, and must fail-closed on an untracked RETRACT claim (no stable id → cannot prove not-new → veto), exactly as gateClaimTransition already does.
- **verify:** Add an adversarial fixture reproducing the laundering swap: before = one claim at 5-rung over-claim (RETRACT); after = that claim resolved honestly BUT a fresh different-id claim introduced at 5-rung over-claim; assert the swap is now REJECTED (today the convex-sum field is conserved at 25→25 and the swap ACCEPTS). Add the Pythagorean 3²+4²=5² partition fixture and assert reject. Re-run the full claim-cortex.test.mjs + claim-ledger.test.mjs green, and the energy-simulate gate grader to confirm falseReject does not spike on healthy scenarios.

- **what:** Three stale narratives all claim the cortex is unwired and only 5/11 terms fire. Runtime proof shows the ledger→cortex bridge is live and 9/11 terms fire. Fix: (a) circuitry-graph claim-cortex node triggeredBy/description (says 'NO live hook consumer', 'live tick still uses 5 of 11 terms'); (b) claim-cortex.mjs:8-29 header ('the gate only ever exercised ~5/11 terms', lists α/β/ε/ζ as 'never fire from real work'); (c) the cortex node should split into two truths — the lightweight LEDGER PROXY (claim-ledger.mjs) IS live and lights α/β/ε; the cortex's own VETO path (gateClaimTransition/maxLadderInversion/θ) is the part still unwired (the real dormancy, tracked by ENG-02). Per the continuity law, any change to what the circuitry represents must propagate to graph + manual + reverify + reindex.
- **agent scope:** 1) Edit the claim-cortex node in yuri-circuitry-graph.json: change description to state the ledger proxy (claim-ledger.mjs) is the LIVE consumer lighting α/β/ε via cortexSnapshot in tickAndTrace, and scope the 'unwired' claim to ONLY the veto path (gateClaimTransition + maxLadderInversion + the θ structural floor, which claimGateFields deliberately omits). 2) Add a claim-ledger node (or edge) if the graph has none — verify with jq whether claim-ledger.mjs is represented at all; it is the load-bearing bridge and may be a missing node. 3) Update the claim-cortex.mjs:8-29 header to past-tense the pre-bridge state and point at claim-ledger.mjs as the wire. 4) Re-run the circuitry render/manual regen per the continuity law and reindex (ai reindex).
- **verify:** jq the updated node and confirm it no longer asserts '5 of 11' or 'NO live hook consumer'. Grep claim-cortex.mjs header for '5/11' and confirm it is reframed. Confirm a claim-ledger node/edge exists in the graph (jq '.nodes[] | select(.files[]|test("claim-ledger"))'). Re-run the live tickAndTrace term-fire check and paste the 9-terms-fired evidence into the node provenance.

### ENG-04 — Kalman/NIS adaptive surprise band with Q-driven recovery (replace the MAD CRITICAL-exclusion hand-patch)
`math-transfer · effort M · risk med · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** replace-hand-tuned-constant  ·  **transfers:** catalog card 1 (Kalman/NIS → adaptive surprise band)
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/energy-tick-core.mjs:122 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/energy-tick-core.mjs:281 · _SYSTEM/SELF/energy-weights.json:52 · 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/math-theory-transfer-catalog-2026-06-03.md:1
- **why now:** The CRITICAL-exclusion hand-patch is exactly the kind of magic-constant scar tissue this sweep targets; recovery dynamics are a genuine new capability the MAD band lacks.
- **what:** energy-tick-core.isSurprise/surpriseEngaged use a static median+K·MAD band, and the code carries an ad-hoc hand-patch (lines 281-291) that EXCLUDES CRITICAL |ΔU| from the band because a protected-path eta=100 spike desensitizes MAD for ~10 ticks. The Kalman transfer tracks log|ΔU| with a scalar filter and fires a one-sided high-tail NIS gate; its real win is Q-driven recovery dynamics — re-sensitize within ~2 ticks of a CRITICAL spike where MAD stays numb ~10 — which RETIRES the CRITICAL-exclusion hand-patch entirely. Q (phase-drift) and R (per-sample noise) become cockpit knobs in energy-weights.json salience block.
- **agent scope:** 1) Implement scalarKalmanSurprise(prevEstimate, prevVar, absDeltaU, Q, R) → {nextEstimate, nextVar, nis, surprised} operating on log|ΔU| with a one-sided high-tail NIS test. 2) DO NOT trust the textbook χ²(1)=3.84 threshold (gating contracts the innovation covariance per arXiv 2512.18508) — make the threshold a tuned knob, recalibrated on a real energy-trace replay. 3) Pull a real ΔU trace from _SYSTEM/state/energy-trace/*.jsonl and replay through BOTH isSurprise(MAD) and the NIS gate; tune Q/R so NIS recovers within ~2 ticks where MAD stays numb ~10. 4) Ship behind the salience knob block with Q/R/nisThreshold defaults equal to a calibrated value, and keep isSurprise as the regression baseline until the replay proves the NIS gate flags genuine phase-changes the MAD band misses. 5) Once shipped, delete the CRITICAL-exclusion branch (the propagated S handles it).
- **verify:** Replay harness over a real trace: assert the NIS gate re-fires within ~2 ticks after a synthetic eta=100 CRITICAL spike where MAD stays numb for ~10. Property test: on a flat in-control stream the NIS false-positive rate matches target. Keep a canary asserting NIS≈MAD on a stationary band so the change is provably additive, not a behavior swap.

### ENG-05 — CUSUM slow-rot regime alarm on the SIGNED ΔU stream
`new-mechanism · effort M · risk low · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** read-lower-bound-not-point, compose-readonly-analyzer  ·  **transfers:** catalog card 2 (CUSUM → slow-rot regime alarm)
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/energy-tick-core.mjs:289 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/energy-tick-core.mjs:135 · 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/math-theory-transfer-catalog-2026-06-03.md:14
- **why now:** Slow-drift is a verified blind spot — the gate literally discards ΔU sign today, so a thread silently tipping into regress is invisible to every existing detector.
- **what:** The surprise band (MAD or Kalman) catches SHOCKS but is blind to slow rot — a thread drifting from progressing to degrading where no single step is an outlier. The recentAbs window throws away ΔU sign on entry (energy-tick-core.mjs:289-291), so the gate cannot see directional drift at all. Add a one-sided UPPER CUSUM on the SIGNED ΔU stream to declare a REGIME CHANGE and escalate to deep-eval / surface to Marcel. MAD catches the shock; CUSUM catches the slow rot — complementary detectors.
- **agent scope:** 1) Add cusum(signedDeltaUStream, k, h) → {alarm, S_t, changeIndex}, S_t = max(0, S_{t-1} + (x_t − μ₀ − k)). 2) Feed it the SIGNED ΔU (add a recentSigned window alongside recentAbs — reuse recentAbs ONLY to source scale: k=0.5·running-MAD, h=5·MAD keeps the dial scale-free, honoring the no-fixed-ΔU-number discipline). 3) UPPER-only — drop the lower arm. 4) Make μ₀ phase-adaptive (recent median signed ΔU, ideally per nen-phase) to kill non-stationarity false-alarms. 5) On alarm, set a deepEngaged-style flag and surface a regime-change advisory; do NOT block (this is a trigger, not a veto). 6) Persist the CUSUM S_t in the session snapshot alongside recentAbs.
- **verify:** Synthetic trace drifting −0.3→+0.3 over 40 ticks where NO single step is a MAD outlier: assert isSurprise() never fires but cusum() alarms and pins changeIndex near onset. Then a flat in-control stream across many seeds: confirm empirical ARL₀ matches target (the false-alarm dial is real).

### ENG-06 — Maximum-Entropy belief prior — retire the magic beliefWidth constant in the cortex
`math-transfer · effort M · risk low · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** replace-hand-tuned-constant  ·  **transfers:** catalog card 28 (MaxEnt → magic-constant-free belief prior), catalog card 36 (Jeffrey conditioning) is the within-tier reliability companion
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/claim-cortex.mjs:289 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/claim-cortex.mjs:303 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/math-kernel.mjs:147 · 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/math-theory-transfer-catalog-2026-06-03.md:95
- **why now:** Removes the last unjustified hand-tuned constant in the cortex belief model with a principled, auditable replacement — directly serves the hallucination-gated 'assumptions visible' discipline.
- **what:** claim-cortex.beliefWidth uses an unjustified hand-tuned law width = LADDER_N/(2·(n+1)) (claim-cortex.mjs:303-306), and beliefVector is a Gaussian-on-rungs stand-in (claim-cortex.mjs:289-296). Replace with a MaxEnt prior maxEntBelief(meanRank, supportSize): solve p_i ∝ exp(λ·i) by a 1-D Newton step on λ to hit Σ i·p_i = meanRank, keep the 1e-9 clamp. Prior = MaxEnt at claimedRank, posterior = MaxEnt at evidenceRank; width now DERIVES from λ + support rather than a magic constant. Makes the ε/info-gain credit across claims MaxEnt-principled and the assumptions AUDITABLE.
- **agent scope:** 1) Add maxEntBelief(meanRank, support) to math-kernel.mjs returning p_i ∝ exp(λ·i) with λ Newton-solved (1-D, 6-rung support is trivial/stable), keeping the 1e-9 clamp for KL/entropy safety. 2) Swap it behind beliefVector in claim-cortex (prior at claimedRank, posterior at evidenceRank), retiring beliefWidth. 3) Honest scope: this hardens the ALREADY-bounded info-gain term's relative ordering and removes a magic constant — it does NOT fix a broken energy (infoGainCeiling log(n) already caps shape-leak). Frame the commit accordingly.
- **verify:** 1) entropy(maxEntBelief) ≥ entropy(currentGaussianBelief) at matched mean (MaxEnt is entropy-maximal for its constraint — if not, impl is wrong). 2) Re-run claim-cortex worked-example and confirm a sharp evidence-confirmed claim yields MORE [0,1]-normalized info-gain because its prior is now genuinely diffuse. 3) Full claim-cortex.test.mjs stays green.

`wiring · effort L · risk low · auto` · deps:

- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-energy-trace.mjs:261 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-energy-trace.mjs:321 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-action-mode-study.mjs · 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/math-theory-transfer-catalog-2026-06-03.md:27

- **what:** Three high-value catalog transfers (card 11 OCO regret receipt — the one Marcel asked for in spirit; card 12 conformal ASSERT bar; card 18 phase-2 Cox β fit) are ALL fenced behind a join that does not exist today: a deferred-outcome record keyed by runId resolving 'did the gated claim get promoted-and-survive / did the protected-path edit get reverted'. The energy-trace is write-once with no later outcome label. Build that labeler as the shared substrate. This is pure-additive observability (a second JSONL), zero live-gate change.
- **agent scope:** 1) Extend the trace schema with a deferred-outcome record type written to a SECOND JSONL (energy-trace-outcomes/<date>.jsonl) keyed by runId — MUST pass the same Privacy Gate (numeric/closed-set only; add any new string field to ALLOWED_STRING_PATHS deliberately). 2) Define a resolver: a later signal (claim promoted and survived N ticks; protected-path edit reverted within session; failed Bash that was later fixed) writes {runId, outcome: 0|1, resolvedAt}. 3) Provide a join utility readJoinedDecisions() that left-joins decisions to outcomes for replay. 4) Reuse action-mode-study's replay plumbing rather than duplicating it. Keep it read/append-only; no gate behavior changes.
- **verify:** Unit-test the join on a seeded trace+outcome pair, asserting unresolved decisions are excluded and resolved ones carry their label. Privacy-gate canary: round-trip a malicious outcome record with a secret in a non-allow-listed field and assert appendTrace throws (serialize-then-re-validate canary still fires). Confirm zero change to live tickAndTrace output (the labeler is a separate writer).

### ENG-08 — Fix the trace privacy-gate canonical-label drift (operator_validated silently dropped)
`drift-fix · effort S · risk med · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** gate-on-identity-not-aggregate  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-energy-trace.mjs:207 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-energy-sanitize.mjs:120 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-energy-hardening.test.mjs:134 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/claim-cortex.mjs:75
- **why now:** Silent telemetry loss at a real rung corrupts any future weight-learning/calibration replay (ENG-07/card 11) that reads the trace, and the current drift test gives false confidence by checking the two drifted sets against each other.
- **what:** CANONICAL_PROMOTION_LABELS in yuri-energy-trace.mjs:207-209 is [draft, research, fixture_ready, runtime_tested, trusted] and OMITS operator_validated, which is a live rung in the cortex LADDER (claim-cortex.mjs:75). summarizeState's closed-set projection (trace.mjs:236) iterates only the canonical set, so any claimPromotionDistribution mass at operator_validated is silently dropped from on-disk telemetry. The existing drift test (yuri-energy-hardening.test.mjs:134) only asserts trace==sanitize labels — both omit it, so the test is green while both drift from the actual ladder. The closed-set is a security control (blocks secret-key smuggling), so the fix must keep it closed AND complete.
- **agent scope:** 1) Decide the canonical source-of-truth: the safest fix is to DERIVE CANONICAL_PROMOTION_LABELS (and sanitize's PROMOTION_LADDER_LABELS) from the cortex LADDER (claim-integrity-gate PROMOTION_STATES) at module load, so the closed set can never drift from the ladder again — but verify there is no import cycle (trace↔sanitize already avoid one; importing claim-integrity-gate's PROMOTION_STATES is acyclic). 2) If a single-source import is rejected for the privacy boundary, instead add operator_validated to BOTH label sets explicitly and STRENGTHEN the drift test to assert the trace canonical set equals the cortex LADDER (not just equals sanitize). 3) Keep 'deprecated' OUT (it is a sink, not a distribution rung — assert that exclusion explicitly).
- **verify:** Add a test asserting [...CANONICAL_PROMOTION_LABELS] equals [...claim-cortex.LADDER] (the assertion that would have caught this). Run a cortexSnapshot containing an operator_validated claim through traceGateEvaluation and assert the on-disk record's claimPromotionDistribution retains the operator_validated count (today it is dropped). Re-run yuri-energy-hardening.test.mjs + yuri-energy-sanitize.test.mjs green.

### ENG-09 — KKT slackness dead-term prune readout over the live trace
`dormancy · effort S · risk low · auto` · deps: ENG-01

- **patterns:** compose-readonly-analyzer  ·  **transfers:** catalog card 32 (Lagrangian/KKT slackness → dead-term prune + legibility)
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-energy-dashboard-data.mjs · _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-energy-trace.mjs:278 · 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/math-theory-transfer-catalog-2026-06-03.md:109
- **why now:** Cheap, read-only, ~80 lines, and it converts this sweep's 'which terms never fire' finding into a durable empirical readout that guides every future term add/prune decision in the layer.
- **what:** The trace already serializes componentContributions + weights per decision. Reinterpret each soft structural term in computeU as a priced constraint and its weighted contribution as an empirical activation cost: any soft term with ~0 cumulative contribution across N real decisions is a documented prune/merge candidate, and every reject can be rendered as a ranked list of binding constraints ('protected-path active cost 100; ladder-inversion active cost 10') instead of an opaque U number. This sweep already has the prediction: staleness(ζ) and malformedForecast(λ) are the rarely/never-binding terms (verified: both never fired in a real WORK run). A read-only analyzer turns that hunch into evidence and gives the gate legibility.
- **agent scope:** 1) Write a read-only Node script over _SYSTEM/state/energy-trace/*.jsonl that, per decision, logs which structural terms were non-zero and their weighted contribution, then ranks constraints by activation-frequency × contribution. 2) Render a per-reject 'binding constraints' readout (dual readout). 3) KEEP the hard vetoes (η protected-path, θ ladder-floor) explicitly flagged as BARRIER terms (non-offsettable) — the slackness/prune lens applies ONLY to offsettable soft terms; do NOT mark a barrier as prunable just because it rarely fires. 4) Emit a prune-candidate list (predicted: ζ, λ). 5) Do NOT claim duality SETS the weights — it does not.
- **verify:** Run over a real trace and confirm protectedPath + ladderInversion dominate activation and staleness/entropy rarely bind, matching the catalog prediction and this sweep's live-fire evidence. Assert the script is read-only (no writes to _SYSTEM/state). Cross-check the prune candidates against ENG-01: if ENG-01 resurrects ζ, ζ should DROP off the prune list — a built-in consistency check between the two items.

### ENG-10 — Bregman/Mahalanobis trust-weighted drift metric for the KL term
`math-transfer · effort M · risk med · OWNER-GATED` · deps: 

- **patterns:** read-lower-bound-not-point, replace-hand-tuned-constant  ·  **transfers:** catalog card 24 (Bregman/mirror descent → trust-weighted drift metric)
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-energy.mjs:157 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-energy.mjs:342 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/math-kernel.mjs:27 · 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/math-theory-transfer-catalog-2026-06-03.md:68
- **why now:** Sharpens the gate's distrust exactly where over-claims are most dangerous (trusted rungs) — but correctly gated behind a cheap-alternative check so it does not add code that simpler per-rung scaling would cover.
- **what:** The β·klDivergence drift term in computeU penalizes claimed-vs-verified drift uniformly across ladder rungs. Generalize it to a swappable Bregman potential ψ so over-claim drift on TRUSTED/runtime_tested rungs is penalized harder than on research rungs (a finite-at-zero metric that also sidesteps the clampDistribution hole). The honest gate: before building, check whether plain per-rung scaling of w.beta gets 80% of this at 10% the code — if so, Bregman is nice-to-have, not load-bearing.
- **agent scope:** Owner-gated (changes the live drift penalty shape). 1) First do the cheap-alternative check: prototype per-rung w.beta scaling and measure how much of the trust-weighting it captures on the over-claim fixtures; if ≥80%, STOP and ship that instead. 2) If Bregman is justified, add bregmanDivergence(p,q,ψ) to math-kernel.mjs with ψ ∈ {negEntropy (MUST reproduce klDivergence to the digit — the regression canary), mahalanobis(W)}. DROP halfSqEuclid (symmetric = wrong for asymmetric over-claim). 3) Set W scaled by ladder-rung trust. 4) Keep clampDistribution semantics or supersede them with the finite-at-zero ψ.
- **verify:** Property test: bregman(negEntropy) === klDivergence on 50 random simplex pairs (exact canary). Run computeU on the veto-mismatch + infogain-buyback fixtures with mahalanobis ψ weighting trusted-rung drift 5×; confirm it raises U strictly MORE than KL on known over-claim fixtures while staying finite on zero-support adversarial cases. Re-run energy-simulate gate grader to confirm falseReject does not regress.

## Drift findings (7)
- claim-cortex node says 'NO live hook consumer... live tick still uses 5 of 11 terms' — STALE. Live runtime proof (tickAndTrace replay of a real Edit/Bash WORK sequence) shows 9 of 11 contribution terms fire: entropy(α), klDivergence(β), logLoss(γ), brier(δ), repeatedFailure(κ), informationGain(ε), protectedPathViolations(η), promotionLadderInversions(θ key, value 0), verifiedEvidenceCredit(ι). The cortex MODULE's own veto path (gateClaimTransition + maxLadderInversion L∞ signal) is still unwired, but the 'cortex has no consumer / 5-of-11' framing is wrong. _SYSTEM/Scripts/claim-cortex.mjs:5, 54; 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/yuri-circuitry-graph.json (claim-cortex node)

# AREA 2: Memory & Subconscious (circuitry layer)

**Top priority:** MEM-01 (ignite the cold-start deadlock) — paired with its prerequisite MEM-02 (mtime fix) and the pre-ignition safety MEM-04 (journal exclusion). The single highest-leverage truth in this layer: every memory organ is individually correct and well-tested, but the assembled subconscious is INERT. Verified live: cold store = 0 rows, usage ledger does not exist, the recall hook is existsSync-gated on the empty cold DB, demote=0 on every dry-run (legitimately, because max file age is 17.8d and mtime resets on checkout), and the daily consolidator plist passes no --execute flag so the forgetting loop can never fire unattended. The result is a self-reinforcing deadlock: empty cold store → no recall → no ledger → mtime-only scoring → demote=0 → cold store stays empty. Nothing downstream (testing effect, re-promotion, prior-turn-lag recall, the four math-transfer cards 14/29/30/31) can demonstrate value until the store is seeded once. Ignition is the unlock; everything else is improving a machine that hasn't been switched on. It is owner-gated because it is the first real execution of forgetting on Marcel's actual memory — but it must be done behind a dry-run demote-set diff for approval, not skipped.

## Improvements

### MEM-01 — Ignite the cold-start deadlock: seed the cold store so the recall→ledger→reinforce loop can bootstrap
`wiring · effort M · risk med · OWNER-GATED` · deps: MEM-02

- **patterns:** shared-prerequisite-unlock  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/kagami-memory-consolidator.mjs:134 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/memory-relocator.mjs:167 · .claude/hooks/user-prompt-submit.js:82
- **why now:** Every other improvement in this layer is downstream of a non-empty cold store. While cold=0 the recall organ, the re-promotion path, the testing effect, and the prior-turn-lag hook are all dead weight. This is the single unlock.
- **what:** The entire subconscious loop (recall, ledger, FSRS demotion, re-promotion, testing-effect) is built, tested, and INERT because the cold store has 0 rows and every entry-point is empty-gated. Break the deadlock with a one-time seed pass: run the consolidator's runSubconsciousPass once with execute=true under a deliberately RELAXED rFloor (e.g. 0.75) OR a tier-age override, so the genuinely-dormant tail (project resume anchors, old session-resume-*.md, superseded feedback) demotes into cold and the store goes non-empty. Once non-empty, user-prompt-submit.js:82's existsSync gate opens, recall fires per turn, the ledger lazy-creates, and the loop is self-sustaining. Pair with MEM-02 (mtime fix) so the seed targets true dormancy not checkout-fresh files. This is owner-gated (first real forgetting execution) — produce a dry-run demote-set diff for owner approval BEFORE flipping execute.
- **agent scope:** 1) Add a `--seed` CLI mode to memory-relocator.mjs that runs planRelocations with an injectable rFloor and prints the full demote-set (slug, R, age-days, tier, body-size) as a table WITHOUT executing. 2) Run it at rFloor 0.6/0.7/0.75 and capture the three demote-sets. 3) Present the diff to owner. 4) On approval, run `node memory-relocator.mjs --execute` (or the consolidator with YURI_SUBCONSCIOUS_EXECUTE=1) once. 5) Verify: `node memory-cold-store.mjs count` returns cold>0, then submit a test prompt whose cue matches a demoted slug and confirm a `<subconscious-recall>` block appears one turn later and _SYSTEM/state/memory-usage.jsonl now exists with a recall event.
- **verify:** Before: memory-cold-store count → {cold:0}; ledger absent. After: cold>0; ledger exists; a cue-matched prompt surfaces a recall block on the next turn; relocation-index.json has non-empty .relocated with reversible relocated/ copies present.

### MEM-02 — Fix the dormancy signal: stop scoring forgetting off file mtime (checkout-fragile), use content-hash-stable last-touch
`harden · effort M · risk med · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** replace-hand-tuned-constant, read-lower-bound-not-point  ·  **transfers:** math-theory-transfer-catalog card 29 (renewal-rate stability)
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/memory-relocator.mjs:93 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/memory-relocator.mjs:117
- **why now:** demote=0 is the headline drift. Without a checkout-stable signal the forgetting loop is non-deterministic across machines and resets itself every reindex — the cold store can never accumulate the dormant tail.
- **what:** The relocator's age prior is fs.statSync(file).mtimeMs (memory-relocator.mjs:117), used as lastUsedMs when the usage ledger has no entry (buildItem:93). mtime is reset by git checkout, reindex, any rewrite, and `claude-memory-write reindex` — so a 60-day-dormant memory looks fresh after any repo operation, and demote=0 is partly an artifact of recent file churn, not true retrievability. Replace the mtime prior with a stable last-touch: prefer the ledger; else fall back to a persisted per-slug first-seen timestamp stored in a sidecar (or git-log author-date of the last CONTENT change via `git log -1 --format=%at -- <file>`, which survives checkout). Keep the mtime path only as a last-resort tertiary. Add a quality/length floor so empty/garbled files don't masquerade as fresh.
- **agent scope:** 1) Add `lastContentChangeMs(file)` helper: try `git log -1 --format=%at -- <relPath>` (×1000), fall back to a `_SYSTEM/state/memory-first-seen.json` sidecar populated lazily, fall back to mtime. 2) In buildItem, use ledger lastUsedMs → lastContentChangeMs → nowMs (drop bare mtime as the primary prior). 3) Unit test: a file touched by checkout but content-unchanged for 60d must score as 60d-old, not 0d. 4) Re-run the dry-run and diff the demote-set vs the mtime baseline.
- **verify:** New unit test in memory-relocator.test.mjs: synthetic file, mtime=now but git-author-date=60d-ago → buildItem.lastUsedMs reflects 60d. Dry-run demote-set changes (more dormant items surface) vs the all-fresh mtime baseline.

### MEM-03 — Wire the testing effect: persist reinforce + bumpStability on recall (kill the dead-code drift)
`wiring · effort M · risk low · auto` · deps: MEM-01

- **patterns:** shared-prerequisite-unlock  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-recall.mjs:69 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-fsrs.mjs:93 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/memory-relocator.mjs:151
- **why now:** The docstrings across three modules promise the testing effect as the system's signature; shipping it as dead code is exactly the prose-outruns-wiring drift the floor forbids. Cheap to wire once MEM-01 makes recall fire.
- **what:** bumpStability (yuri-fsrs.mjs:93) and the 'reinforce' event (memory-usage.mjs:30) are exported, documented as the live spacing/testing-effect mechanism, and have NO production caller. recall() (yuri-recall.mjs:69) records only a 'recall' event and never strengthens stability — so a frequently-recalled cold memory does NOT decay slower, contradicting the module's core claim. Wire it: on a successful recall that crosses a confidence/rank threshold, (a) write a 'reinforce' event (already increments lastReinforcedMs in buildUsageIndex:63) and (b) when a re-promotion happens, apply bumpStability to the restored memory's effective base stability so it survives longer before the next demotion. This makes effectiveStability's freq term actually compound across recalls.
- **agent scope:** 1) In recall(), after recordUse('recall'), emit a 'reinforce' event for any surfaced slug whose score exceeds a reinforceThreshold (sourced from energy-weights recall block). 2) In promoteHot(), after restoring the body, compute bumped base-stability via bumpStability(item.baseStabilityDays) and write it back into the restored file's frontmatter (or a stability sidecar) so the next demotion pass sees the strengthened S. 3) Unit test: recall the same slug 3× → its effectiveStability strictly increases and its R at fixed t rises. 4) Confirm energy-weights-drift.test still passes (knob additions).
- **verify:** New test: 3 reinforce events for a slug raise its useCount-driven effectiveStability above the 0-recall baseline; promoteHot writes a strictly-larger base stability. grep confirms bumpStability now has a non-test caller.

### MEM-05 — Card 14: Minimum Description Length → redundancy-aware demotion (protect-only marginal-bits)
`math-transfer · effort L · risk med · auto` · deps: MEM-02

- **patterns:** read-lower-bound-not-point, gate-on-identity-not-aggregate  ·  **transfers:** math-theory-transfer-catalog card 14
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/memory-relocator.mjs:98 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-fsrs.mjs:73
- **why now:** MEMORY.md overflow + 129 files means redundancy is the real governance problem, not just age. This is the highest-juice memory card and lands directly in the function the relocator already exposes for testing.
- **what:** Catalog card 14 (juice 7, confidence HIGH) targets exactly planRelocations/evaluateRetention. Today demotion is pure FSRS retrievability — a stale-but-UNIQUE memory and a fresh-but-DUPLICATE memory are scored only by recall decay. Add a marginalBits(item, restOfStore) term: a memory carrying high marginal description-length (lexically novel vs the store) is PROTECTED from demotion even if stale; a memory whose content is near-fully predicted by the rest is FLAGGED redundant. Strict guards per the card: (1) force_keep/feedback/user stay fully exempt; (2) marginal-bits may only PROTECT-from-demote or FLAG-redundant, NEVER sole-delete; (3) add a quality/length floor so a junk/garbled low-content file can't false-protect itself as 'novel'.
- **agent scope:** Implement marginalBits via an embedding-free token-set surprisal (e.g. fraction of a file's tokens not covered by the union of the rest, or a simple compression-ratio proxy gzip(item)+gzip(rest) vs gzip(item+rest)). Extend planRelocations with an AND condition (demote only if low-R AND low-marginal-bits). Unit-test three synthetic stores per the card: (1) stale+unique → KEPT (regression vs today's pure-FSRS demote), (2) fresh+duplicate → FLAGGED redundant, (3) low-quality lexically-novel junk → must NOT be protected (quality floor). Dry-run vs the live root, diff demote-set against the FSRS-only baseline.
- **verify:** Three new unit tests pass exactly as the card specifies. Dry-run demote-set diff shows stale+unique items now retained and fresh+duplicate items flagged, vs the FSRS-only baseline.

### MEM-06 — Card 29 Half-1: renewal-RATE stability (frequency-per-time beats raw count)
`math-transfer · effort M · risk low · auto` · deps: MEM-03

- **patterns:** replace-hand-tuned-constant  ·  **transfers:** math-theory-transfer-catalog card 29 (Half 1 only)
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-fsrs.mjs:55
- **why now:** Low-risk high-leverage stability fix that directly improves which dormant items the (now-igniting) loop demotes; reuses the ledger MEM-03 starts populating.
- **what:** Card 29 (juice 6, structural HIGH for Half 1) targets effectiveStability. Today freq boosts stability via freq·log1p(useCount) (yuri-fsrs.mjs:59) — raw count, which conflates a memory recalled 5× in one day with one recalled 5× over a year. Replace with a renewal-rate term: r = useCount / max(elapsedDays, minWindow); boost S by freq·log1p(r·SCALE), so frequency-PER-TIME drives durability. Sourced from the existing usage ledger, no new logging, with an mtime/first-seen-prior fallback for items with <4 recalls. SHIP ONLY Half 1 — explicitly FENCE the inspection-paradox debias (Half 2) which the card warns rescues dead memories without a stationarity guard.
- **agent scope:** 1) Extend effectiveStability to accept firstSeenMs/elapsedDays; compute r = useCount/max(elapsedDays, minWindow). 2) Replace freq·log1p(useCount) with freq·log1p(r·SCALE); SCALE + minWindow as fsrs knobs in energy-weights. 3) For items with <4 recalls, assert fallback to the count-based or mtime prior (don't trust a rate from 1 sample). 4) A/B planRelocations: log1p(useCount) vs renewal-rate; diff demote-sets, flag length-bias false-positives the old path demoted. Read-only diff first. DO NOT implement Half 2 (inspection-paradox debias) — leave a parked-branch comment citing the card's stationarity-guard risk.
- **verify:** Unit test: two slugs same useCount, one over 1 day vs one over 100 days → the dense-recall slug gets higher S. A/B dry-run shows the length-bias false-positives the card predicts. grep confirms NO Half-2 debias code shipped.

### MEM-09 — Bound MEMORY.md: tiered/summarized reindex with eviction so the index stops overflowing the load limit
`harden · effort M · risk low · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** read-lower-bound-not-point  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/claude-memory-write.mjs:200 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/claude-memory-write.mjs:242
- **why now:** The index is already over the limit and truncating TODAY — the brain is loading a partial memory index every session. Self-inflicted continuity loss, which the persona floor calls broken trust.
- **what:** The SessionStart reindex (claude-memory-write.mjs:242) rewrites the WHOLE index, one full line per file, no cap — at 129 entries it is 26253 bytes, already over the 24985-byte (24.4KB) runtime load limit, and the runtime silently truncates it (confirmed: this session's own context warning loaded only part of MEMORY.md). It grows unbounded. Make the index self-bounding: (1) exclude demoted/relocated slugs (they live in cold, not hot); (2) tier the index — full lines for feedback/user/active-project, compressed one-liners or a roll-up section for stale references; (3) hard-cap total bytes under the limit with an overflow roll-up ('+N older entries — query via ai search'). Keep the file machine-parseable.
- **agent scope:** 1) In the reindex builder, read relocation-index.json and skip relocated slugs. 2) Sort entries by tier/recency; emit full lines until a byte budget (e.g. 22KB) is hit, then collapse the tail into a single roll-up line with a count. 3) Truncate each description to a hard char cap (the existing warning already says keep under ~200 chars). 4) Verify the written MEMORY.md is < 24985 bytes.
- **verify:** After reindex, `wc -c MEMORY.md` < 24985; relocated slugs absent from the index; the runtime no longer emits the overflow warning. A demoted-then-promoted slug re-appears in the index after promoteHot.

## Drift findings (8)
- DEAD CODE: bumpStability() (yuri-fsrs.mjs:93) and the 'reinforce' event (memory-usage.mjs:30) have ZERO production callers — only tests. The testing-effect/expanding-interval strengthening the module docstrings promise (yuri-fsrs.mjs:8-9, memory-usage.mjs:7, yuri-recall.mjs:11) is never wired: recall() only writes 'recall' events and never calls bumpStability to persist a strengthened stability. The 'spacing effect made real' claim is aspirational, not live.
- COLD-START DEADLOCK (verified, not a code bug but a system-level drift): cold store holds 0 rows (memory-cold-store count → cold:0), the usage ledger _SYSTEM/state/memory-usage.jsonl does NOT exist (lazy-create on first recall), and yuri-recall's spawn is gated `if (!fs.existsSync(COLD_DB_FILE)) return false` (user-prompt-submit.js:82). So: empty cold store → recall never fires → ledger never written → relocator scores off mtime only → demote=0 → cold store stays empty. The loop cannot bootstrap itself. Every module is individually correct; the assembled organ is inert.
- demote=0 DECODED (verified via live dry-run: scanned=129 demoted=0): NOT broken — legitimate. Max memory-file age is 17.8d (median 4.3d); the smallest tier (working=3d) at 18d with useCount 0 yields R≈0.65 > rFloor 0.6, so nothing crosses. But the age signal is FILE MTIME (memory-relocator.mjs:117), which git-checkout / reindex / any rewrite resets — so true dormancy is masked. The relocator can demote on a clean checkout only by accident.
- consolidator runs DRY-RUN FOREVER: the plist com.yuri.kagami-memory-consolidator.plist ProgramArguments invoke the script with NO --execute flag and YURI_SUBCONSCIOUS_EXECUTE is unset (verified), and main() reads execute from exactly those two sources (kagami-memory-consolidator.mjs:203). The scheduled daily organ can never fire the forgetting loop unattended — it only logs candidates. Matches the node description but means the cold store can never populate via the scheduled path.
- JOURNAL BLOAT IN SCORING SET (verified): session-journal.md is 1,253,585 bytes and IS loaded by loadItems() into a scoring item with its FULL 1.25MB body in RAM (verified: bodyLen 1253585, only file >50KB), baseStab=14, useCount=0. It is operational telemetry, not a recallable memory. If it ever demotes it gets copied verbatim as a single 1.25MB FTS5 row into the cold store; meanwhile it inflates every dry-run pass and the MLX consolidator prompt.
- MEMORY.md OVERFLOW IS STRUCTURAL: index body is 26253 bytes vs the 24985-byte (24.4KB) load limit — the SessionStart reindex (claude-memory-write.mjs:242) rewrites the ENTIRE index unconditionally with one line per file (129 entries / 129 files), no eviction, no summarization, no demoted-file exclusion. It grows unbounded with file count; the runtime already truncates it (only part loaded — confirmed in this session's own context warning).
- MLX consolidator prompt unbounded: analyzeMemories (kagami-memory-consolidator.mjs:70-89) joins ALL 129 files (300 chars each ≈ 40K chars) into ONE prompt with max_tokens:512 — output cannot enumerate issues across 129 files; the LLM-eyeball stale/dup/contradiction pass is structurally underpowered at this corpus size.
- TRACK-A ROOT vs SUBCONSCIOUS TARGET split is real but undocumented in the node graph: memory-kernel roots Track-A at _SYSTEM/memory (memory-kernel.mjs:13), while the relocator/consolidator subconscious operates ONLY on Track-B ~/.claude/projects/.../memory (memory-relocator.mjs DEFAULT_MEMORY_ROOT, consolidator MEMORY_DIR:23). The FSRS forgetting loop NEVER touches Track-A canonical memory — by design (owner directive 2026-06-02) but the circuitry description doesn't flag that the whole subconscious is Track-B-only.

### RK-05 — Harden buildMatch test coverage against FTS5 operator-keyword injection
`harden · effort S · risk low · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:**   ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-search.test.mjs:19 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-search.mjs:27
- **why now:** Security-critical ingress with a hardening claim that the test suite doesn't actually exercise for the operator-injection class.
- **what:** buildMatch (yuri-search.mjs:27-38) is the injection-hardened ingress: it splits on whitespace and wraps each term in double-quotes, which neutralizes FTS5 operators because a quoted token is a literal. The test (yuri-search.test.mjs:19-27) only checks punctuation specials (foo- bar* (baz)). It does NOT assert that bare FTS5 keyword operators (OR, AND, NOT, NEAR) submitted as user terms are treated as literals, not operators. Add cases proving `buildMatch('cats NOT dogs')` yields '"cats" OR "NOT" OR "dogs"' (NOT quoted-as-literal, not an FTS5 NOT operator) and that a column-filter attempt like `body:secret` is quoted. This locks the security contract the node description claims ('raw user input can never inject FTS5 operators').
- **agent scope:** In yuri-search.test.mjs add checks: (1) buildMatch('cats NOT dogs') === '"cats" OR "NOT" OR "dogs"' (operator demoted to quoted literal); (2) buildMatch('a OR b') stays an OR-of-quoted-terms with 'OR' itself quoted as a term; (3) a round-trip FTS5 query using buildMatch on a malicious-looking input like 'body:secret NEAR/0 x' does not throw and returns only literal matches (extend the existing in-memory db test). Do not change buildMatch itself unless a case fails — the goal is to pin the contract. If the column-filter case (term containing ':') reveals a real bypass, then also strip ':' in the term sanitizer at yuri-search.mjs:36.
- **verify:** node _SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-search.test.mjs shows the new PASS lines and exits 0; deliberately breaking buildMatch (e.g. removing the quote wrap) makes the new tests FAIL (negative check).

# AREA 4: Circuitry layer: Governance & Safety (8 nodes — bash-security-guard, operator-write-guard, yuri-operator, tirith-url-guard, claude-protocol-guard, yuri-risk-lite, lane-kernel; + energy-enforce PEP as adjacent layer-2)

**Top priority:** ENG-01 (bash-security-guard read-gate fail-open) is the single highest-leverage fix. The mutation/push/role-path gates iterate ALL tokens (airtight — verified coworker bypasses all denied), but the .env/sensitive-.claude READ gate anchors only on parts[0] as the command verb. Verified live: 'X=1 cat .env', 'true; cat .env', and 'cat \"$HOME/../...env\"' (absolute/quoted path) ALL bypass the read block and return allow-no-output. This is a genuine protected-secret read bypass in the primary deterministic gate, and it directly contradicts the node's claimed job ('DENIES .env read'). It is a fail-open in the exact bug-bounty class the corpus warns about (broken access control via input-parsing asymmetry).

## Improvements

### ENG-01 — Close the .env / sensitive-.claude READ fail-open in bash-security-guard (compound-cmd, env-prefix, absolute-path bypasses)
`harden · effort M · risk med · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** gate-on-identity-not-aggregate  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** .claude/hooks/bash-security-guard.js:72 · .claude/hooks/bash-security-guard.js:90 · .claude/hooks/bash-security-guard.js:39 · .claude/hooks/bash-security-guard.js:56
- **why now:** Verified live bypass of the primary protected-secret read gate; the node's own description claims this is blocked. Bug-bounty corpus class: broken access control via parser asymmetry.
- **what:** The read-block functions (isBlockedEnvRead, isBlockedSensitiveClaudeRead) inspect only parts[0] as the read command and match .env by exact basename ('.env' | './.env'). Three verified live bypasses: (1) env-var prefix 'X=1 cat .env' shifts the verb off parts[0]; (2) compound 'true; cat .env' is one command string but only the first segment is parsed; (3) absolute/quoted path 'cat "$HOME/../marcelspatz/YURI-OS-MUSUBI/.env"' is not in the exact-match set. The mutation/push gates already do whole-token scans and are airtight — bring the read gates to parity. Fix: (a) split the command on shell separators (; && || | newline) and inspect EACH segment, mirroring how isRolePathMutation/isGitPush already scan all tokens; (b) replace exact-basename .env matching with an endsWithDotEnv()-style check that also catches absolute paths resolving to a repo .env (reuse the existing endsWithDotEnv helper at line 56, already in the file); (c) add dev-credential.json to the sensitive-read set so the trust-root hash is not Bash-readable by a coworker (parity with the Write-side protection). NOTE: the hash is safe-to-commit by design, so (c) is lower severity than (a)/(b).
- **agent scope:** In .claude/hooks/bash-security-guard.js: add splitSegments(cmd) that splits on /\s*(?:;|&&|\|\||\||\n)\s*/ and trims; refactor inspectCommand to run the env/claude READ checks over each segment (mutation checks already scan whole-token so leave them). Generalize isEnvTarget to also return true when unquote(tok) ends in '/.env' OR resolves (path.resolve) to a path ending '/.env' under REPO_ROOT_PREFIX (reuse endsWithDotEnv + isPathInsideRepo already defined lines 52-59). Add '_SYSTEM/SELF/dev-credential.json' to a new BLOCKED_SENSITIVE_FILES set checked in isBlockedSensitiveClaudeRead. Add regression rows to .claude/hooks/tests/bash-security-guard.matrix.test.js for each of the 3 bypass strings (force coworker via wrong YURI_DEV_KEY for the cred case; .env read denies regardless of role).

Run node .claude/hooks/tests/bash-security-guard.matrix.test.js — expect new rows green and existing 130/130 still pass. Negative control: 'cat .env.example' and 'cat README.md' must still allow.

### ENG-04 — Harden operator-write-guard to cover worktree-resolved guard copies (canonicalization scope gap)
`harden · effort M · risk med · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** fail-closed-on-tamper  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** .claude/hooks/operator-write-guard.js:93 · .claude/hooks/operator-write-guard.js:43 · .claude/hooks/operator-write-guard.js:113
- **why now:** Worktrees are live on disk now; the guard's REPO_ROOT anchoring is a real defense-in-depth hole the moment a session runs from a worktree.
- **what:** operator-write-guard.js canonicalizes the target via realpath then matches against PROTECTED_ROLE_FILES anchored at the single REPO_ROOT (path.resolve(__dirname,'..','..')). When the active session cwd is a worktree (.claude/worktrees/<x>/), that worktree has its OWN copy of bash-security-guard.js etc.; their realpath is under the worktree dir, NOT REPO_ROOT, so a coworker Edit of the worktree guard copy is NOT denied. The original guards stay protected, but a worktree session could neuter its local enforcement chain. Verified 3+ worktree copies exist. Fix: match the protected SUFFIX (the repo-relative tail like '.claude/hooks/bash-security-guard.js') against the canonicalized path's tail, in addition to the absolute REPO_ROOT match — so any path ending in a protected control-file tail is denied regardless of which worktree root it lives under.
- **agent scope:** In isProtectedTarget(absPath): in addition to the existing absolute-equality check against PROTECTED_ROLE_FILES, also test whether the lowercased canonical path ENDS WITH any protected repo-relative tail (derive tails by stripping REPO_ROOT from PROTECTED_ROLE_FILES, or keep a parallel PROTECTED_TAILS list of the repo-relative strings already present at lines 44-55). Keep case-insensitive compare. Guard against false positives: only suffix-match the .claude/hooks/* and _SYSTEM/SELF|Scripts/* tails (full repo-relative path including the dir prefix), never a bare basename. Add a regression to operator-write-guard.test.js: a Write to a synthetic '/tmp/fake-worktree/.claude/hooks/bash-security-guard.js' (realpath outside REPO_ROOT) must deny for coworker.
- **verify:** node .claude/hooks/tests/operator-write-guard.test.js — add and pass a worktree-tail case; existing 32 checks stay green. Manually: printf a coworker-forced Write event targeting a worktree copy path returns permissionDecision=deny. Negative: a Write to '/tmp/x/.claude/hooks/token-budget-check.js' (operational, non-enforcement) still allows.

### ENG-05 — tirith-url-guard: default-deny-on-binary-missing is fail-OPEN by default (only fail-loud behind an opt-in flag)
`harden · effort S · risk low · OWNER-GATED` · deps: 

- **patterns:** compose-readonly-analyzer  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** .claude/hooks/tirith-url-guard.js:11 · .claude/hooks/tirith-url-guard.js:62 · .claude/hooks/tirith-url-guard.js:85
- **why now:** The URL threat gate is silently inert in the committed default — a security control that does nothing unless an undocumented env flag is set is a trap.
- **what:** tirith-url-guard.js exits 0 (silent allow) when ~/.hermes/bin/tirith is absent or returns unparseable output, UNLESS TIRITH_FAIL_LOUD=1 is set (default OFF, line 11). So the URL threat gate is a no-op on any machine without the external binary installed — which is the committed default. The node description honestly states this ('A missing binary silently allows unless TIRITH_FAIL_LOUD=1'), so it is documented-not-drift, but it is a fail-open posture on a security gate. Also: the 5s execSync per URL (line 62-63) is an unbounded fan-out — a Bash command embedding N URLs spawns N synchronous 5s subprocesses (DoS-via-resource-consumption corpus class). Recommend: (a) make TIRITH_FAIL_LOUD default ON (ask, not silent-allow, when the gate cannot assess) OR at minimum emit a one-time advisory so the operator knows the gate is inert; (b) cap URLs-per-command (e.g. first 5) and total wall-time budget.
- **agent scope:** Owner-gated because flipping FAIL_LOUD default changes friction. Option A (recommended): change line 11 to default true unless TIRITH_FAIL_LOUD==='0'; keep the existing emitFailLoudAsk path. Option B (minimal): when the binary is missing, emit a single PreToolUse additionalContext advisory ('tirith URL gate inert — binary not installed') instead of silent exit, so the inert gate is visible. Add a URL cap: slice extractUrls() to the first MAX_URLS=5 and track a cumulative deadline (e.g. 8s total) across the loop, breaking with an 'ask' if exceeded. Add a test stub that runs the hook with a non-existent TIRITH_BIN and asserts the chosen behavior.
- **verify:** With tirith absent and the new default: a Bash command containing a URL emits either an 'ask' (Option A) or a visible advisory (Option B), not silent exit 0. A command with 10 URLs spawns at most 5 tirith calls (instrument with a fake tirith that logs invocations). Existing behavior with a present binary scoring SAFE still allows.

Build the synthetic −0.3->+0.3-over-40-ticks trace; confirm isSurprise() never fires but cusum() alarms with changeIndex near onset; confirm ARL0 on a flat in-control stream matches target. Wire alarm -> 'steer'/'probe' breaker decision (advisory escalate-to-deep-eval), NOT an immediate deny. Card #1 (optional second pass): scalarKalmanSurprise() for Q-driven recovery, replacing the ad-hoc CRITICAL-exclusion patch. Ship flag-gated + metrics-only burn-in first (mirror energy-enforce's ENFORCE default-OFF), audit zero false trips, then arm. Owner-gated: touches the energy gate's verdict surface.

- CHAIN-ORDER DRIFT: circuitry node descriptions claim fixed chain positions that do NOT match settings.json. bash-security-guard claims '2nd in chain' (actual 2nd — OK), operator-write-guard claims '3rd' (actual 3rd — OK), claude-protocol-guard claims '5th' (actual 5th — OK), yuri-risk-lite claims '8th' (actual 8th — OK). Positions actually hold, BUT the graph omits pre-tool-gate (1st), pre-tool-use (6th), musubi-protocol-enforce (7th), token-budget-check (9th), energy-enforce (10th) entirely — the Governance layer map is missing 5 of the 10 PreToolUse hooks. .claude/settings.json PreToolUse chain vs 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/yuri-circuitry-graph.json
- PHANTOM FILE: operator-write-guard.js:50 lists '.claude/hooks/claude-protocol-guard.js' in PROTECTED_ROLE_FILES and operator-write-guard.test.js:59 asserts it denies — but only claude-protocol-guard.mjs exists on disk (ls confirms no .js). Forward-looking by design, but it's an untracked phantom: the guard protects a non-existent file while the real .mjs IS also listed (line 51), so net effect is benign. Still a map-vs-reality mismatch worth a comment. .claude/hooks/operator-write-guard.js:50
- MECHANISM-PATTERN UNPOPULATED: all 7 Governance & Safety nodes have mechanismPattern=NONE in the graph, yet the code plainly embodies 'gate-on-identity-not-aggregate' (yuri-operator.cjs resolveRole — role keyed on passphrase identity, not an aggregate score) and a fail-closed variant. The propagation taxonomy is blind to the entire safety layer. 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/yuri-circuitry-graph.json nodes[].mechanismPattern
- CLAUDE.md claims energy circuit-breaker 'BLOCKS ... through the circuit-breaker PreToolUse hook' as armed behavior — verified energy-enforce.mjs:45-48 defaults OFF (needs both YURI_ENERGY_ENFORCE=1 AND the gitignored flag file), and is metrics-only burn-in by default. The CLAUDE.md prose is accurate about the gating but a reader could over-trust it as live; the layer-2 conscience is DORMANT in the committed default. .claude/hooks/energy-enforce.mjs:45 vs CLAUDE.md 'Action / claim evaluation' block
- WORKTREE GUARD COPIES UNGUARDED: 3+ .claude/worktrees/*/.claude/hooks/bash-security-guard.js copies exist (ls confirmed). operator-write-guard canonicalizes via realpath then matches against REPO_ROOT-anchored absolute paths — a worktree copy resolves to a DIFFERENT absolute path, so editing the worktree's guard copy is NOT denied for a coworker. Defense-in-depth gap if a worktree is ever the active cwd. .claude/worktrees/*/.claude/hooks/bash-security-guard.js

# AREA 5: Cognition & Persona (circuitry layer: brain-inject, soul-persona-inject, persona-md, cortex-state, fingerprint)

**Top priority:** COG-01 (sync REQUIRED_HEADINGS to the full persona contract) is the single highest-leverage item: it is a true drift-fix where the live boot brain silently drops 5 of the system's named cognitive-mode anchors (Izanagi/Haki/Nen/Bankai/Geass) that the decoder doc, the anime-DNA skills, and SOUL.md all treat as load-bearing. It is S-effort, low-risk, and it is the prerequisite honesty fix before any decoder-wiring work — there is no point wiring a 12-stage decode pipeline whose Haki/Izanagi stages aren't even guaranteed present in context. It also surfaces the deeper structural defect (a hardcoded heading whitelist that drifts every time SOUL.md grows), which COG-02 generalizes.

## Improvements

SOUL.md has 31 bold ** headings; the brain block injects 9. Either (a) extend the whitelist to include the 5 cognitive-mode rules (minimal), or (b) replace the hardcoded list with a derive-all-** -headings extractor capped/ordered by a small priority list so future SOUL.md growth never silently drops a rule. Prefer (b) with a fail-LOUD canary: emit a one-line stderr warning when SOUL.md has bold headings not represented, so drift is visible not silent. ### COG-02 — Unify the persona-extraction failure contract (fail-closed canary, no silent drop)
`harden · effort S · risk low · auto` · deps: COG-01

- **patterns:** gate-on-identity-not-aggregate  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** .claude/hooks/brain-inject.js:121 · .claude/hooks/soul-persona-inject.js:33 · .claude/hooks/soul-persona-inject.js:68
- **why now:** A persona that silently runs at partial fidelity is the exact 'forgetting is broken trust' failure; the divergence between the two hooks means the same corruption produces different (both invisible) outcomes.
- **what:** brain-inject.js:124-126 silently skips a missing SOUL heading (fail-OPEN: degrades the brain with no signal); soul-persona-inject.js:39-40 throws then exit(0) swallows (effectively also silent at the harness). Neither surfaces drift. Make both fail the SAME way: keep the hook process alive (never block boot) but emit a deterministic machine-parseable stderr line (e.g. `PERSONA_DRIFT missing=<heading>`) AND inject a visible `(persona rule missing: <heading>)` marker into the block so the running session can SEE its own persona is incomplete. This embodies the no-silent-cap / fail-loud weakness-class lesson (bug-bounty corpus: silent degradation hides the defect).
- **agent scope:** 1) In both extractors, on a missing heading push a sentinel string `(MISSING persona rule: <heading>)` instead of skipping, and write `PERSONA_DRIFT missing=<heading>` to stderr. 2) Confirm brain-inject's main() never throws on this path (it must still emit a block). 3) Add a unit assertion in soul-persona-inject's existing module.exports test surface. 4) Verify the harness still boots (exit 0) with a deliberately broken SOUL heading.
- **verify:** Copy SOUL.md to /tmp, delete one heading, point YURI_ROOT at it, run both hooks; confirm stderr carries `PERSONA_DRIFT missing=` AND the block contains the sentinel marker AND exit code is 0 (boot not blocked).

# AREA 6: Learning & Continuity (lifecycle-loop host)

**Top priority:** ENG-LC-01 (wire or retire yuri-health). The graph names yuri-health as this layer's liveness dashboard, but it is a dead-end harness with no caller, no plist, and no persisted output, while two OTHER unmentioned health monitors run on launchd. This is the highest-leverage item because it is simultaneously a drift-fix (graph claim is false), a dormancy fix (built-but-unfired), and a fragmentation fix (3 overlapping health surfaces with no single source of truth) — and health monitoring is the organ that is supposed to PROVE the rest of the system is wired, so its own brokenness is load-bearing.

Folding into neuron-loop is cleaner: it consolidates all overnight self-learning into one scheduled host and the brain-inject LEARNED_RULES section (brain-inject.js:457) already reads global.md, so processed dream rules would surface next session automatically.
- **agent scope:** 1) Confirm dream-queue.jsonl currently has 'pending' rows that never get drained (grep '"status":"pending"' .claude/yuri-sentinel/learning/dream-queue.jsonl). 2) Add a phase to neuron-loop.mjs (e.g. between phase 2 pattern-promoter and phase 3) that runs yuri-dream-processor.mjs via runScript() with a guard for offload.sh availability (DeepSeek may be cold — wrap in the same code!==0 warn pattern used by every other phase, neuron-loop.mjs:224). 3) Add a PATHS.dreamProcessor entry. 4) Verify the processor's --dry-run flag is honored when neuron-loop runs --dry-run (it accepts --dry-run per dream-processor.mjs:10) so the nightly dry-run path stays write-free. 5) Update graph yuri-dream node description to name the processor as the consumer + the new scheduled host; reindex.

The skill-notes path is date-deduped (session-reflect.js:106-115) so it mostly absorbs the second run, but writeJournal (session-reflect.js:165-178) prepends a fresh dated entry with NO idempotency key — the second invocation can write a duplicate journal block, and there is a race: the first run sets state.status='closed' + renames session-state.json (session-reflect.js:201-202) while the second run may have already read the open state. ### ENG-LC-04 — Tier-2 math engine for neuron-loop trend: CUSUM slow-drift + Kalman-recovery over improvement_score
`math-transfer · effort M · risk low · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** read-lower-bound-not-point  ·  **transfers:** Card #2 CUSUM slow-drift, Card #1 Kalman one-sided NIS recovery (deferred follow-up)
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/neuron-loop.mjs:333 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/neuron-loop.mjs:295 · .claude/yuri-sentinel/learning/synthesis.jsonl · _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/math-kernel.mjs
- **why now:** The self-improvement loop measures itself with a memoryless single-step delta — it literally cannot see itself slowly degrading, which is the exact failure mode a self-improvement organ must catch.
- **what:** neuron-loop.mjs:333-343 computes a scalar improvement_score (0-100) and stores flaws_delta / rules_delta as raw single-step diffs vs the immediately prior run (neuron-loop.mjs:295,302). This is a SHOCK-only view: a slow multi-run degradation (score drifting 70->50 over 8 nightly runs, no single step an outlier) is invisible, and a sudden CRITICAL regression has no re-sensitization dynamic. Two catalog cards fit the synthesis.jsonl time-series (43 records exist, real data): Card #2 (CUSUM, juice/confidence HIGH) — add cusum(score_stream, k, h) over synthesis.jsonl to alarm on cumulative slow decline of improvement_score (or flaws_found creep) with a tunable ARL false-alarm dial; Card #1 (Kalman one-sided NIS on log|delta|) — track log of the run-over-run score delta with a scalar Kalman filter for Q-driven recovery after a CRITICAL spike. This turns neuron-loop's flat 'delta vs prior' into a real trend-health organ feeding the NEURON_LOOP brain section (brain-inject.js:433) with a drift verdict, not just a number.
- **agent scope:** 1) Read synthesis.jsonl (43 records) to confirm improvement_score is present and numeric per row. 2) Add cusum(stream, k, h) -> {alarm, S_t, changeIndex} to math-kernel.mjs (the Tier-1 primitive) per Card #2's smallest-experiment, with the synthetic drift test (−0.3→+0.3 over 40 ticks, no single MAD outlier) as the regression canary. 3) In neuron-loop's phase-8 synthesis block, read the last N synthesis.jsonl scores, run cusum, and add a trend_alarm + changeIndex field to the synthesis object. 4) Emit the alarm into the brain-stale sentinel reason (neuron-loop.mjs:359) so a sustained decline reaches the next session. 5) Defer the Kalman card to a follow-up — CUSUM is the high-confidence free win. 6) Empirically pick k,h against the real 43-record trace, not textbook constants (Card #2 mismatch note). 7) Tag mechanismPattern read-lower-bound-not-point (trend lower bound, not point delta).
- **verify:** Unit test: cusum on a synthetic flat-then-drifting score stream alarms at the drift onset and pins changeIndex; cusum on a flat in-control stream gives empirical ARL0 near target across seeds (the false-alarm dial proof). node _SYSTEM/Scripts/neuron-loop.mjs --dry-run emits trend_alarm in the synthesis JSON without writing. Replay the real 43-record synthesis.jsonl and confirm the verdict is sane (no alarm on a stable trace).

### ENG-LC-05 — Harden neuron-loop external-knowledge reads against masked-zero (arxiv derived-path + fail-open phases)
`harden · effort S · risk low · auto` · deps:

- **why now:** A self-improvement loop that cannot tell 'no new papers today' from 'the arxiv arm has been silently dead for two weeks' is lying to its own brain via improvement_score.
- **what:** neuron-loop.mjs:277 reads arxiv papers via a fragile derived filename: PATHS.githubTrending.replace('github-trending','arxiv-pulse') then ?.papers?.length ?? 0. If knowledge-scout never writes arxiv-pulse.json (or the schema drifts to a different key), externalPapers silently collapses to 0 with no signal that the arxiv ingestion arm is dead — a masked-zero that the improvement_score (neuron-loop.mjs:340 externalGain) absorbs without complaint. Every phase also swallows non-zero exits as a logged warning and proceeds (neuron-loop.mjs:224,230,238,...), which is correct fail-open for a nightly job but means a CHRONICALLY broken phase looks identical to a healthy one in the synthesis record. Add a per-phase ok/degraded flag to the synthesis object and a canary: if external_knowledge.signal_gain is 0 for K consecutive runs, set a degraded marker that surfaces in the NEURON_LOOP brain section. - **verify:** Run neuron-loop with arxiv-pulse.json absent -> synthesis.json shows phases.knowledgeScout flag and a degraded marker, NOT a clean externalPapers:0. node --check passes. grep arxivPulse _SYSTEM/Scripts/neuron-loop.mjs shows the explicit PATHS entry (no more 'arxiv-pulse' string-replace).

- **why now:** Three half-wired health monitors is exactly the kind of fragmentation that lets a dead organ (yuri-health) sit unnoticed — one tiered contract makes 'is YURI alive' answerable in one read and is the long-term home for ENG-LC-01.
- **what:** Study-competition lens: mature systems expose ONE health endpoint with tiered depth (liveness/readiness/deep-probe), not three uncoordinated scripts. Today YURI has lane-health.sh (lane reachability), health-aggregator.mjs (launchd + hook coverage + reads lane-health), and yuri-health.mjs (deep circuit probes) — three overlapping monitors, two scheduled, one orphaned, with no single 'is YURI healthy' verdict a human or the brain can read. Define a single tiered contract: tier-0 liveness (process/launchd up = health-aggregator's job), tier-1 readiness (lanes reachable = lane-health.sh), tier-2 deep (memory_governor/energy/skill-registry/gitnexus circuits actually accruing = yuri-health.mjs). health-aggregator becomes the composer that reads tier-1 + tier-2 status files and emits one rollup verdict + the worst-tier reason. brain-inject's laneHealth section (already present per brain-inject.js buildBrainBlock args) then reads the single rollup instead of a partial.
- **agent scope:** 1) Owner-gated design step: agree the tier-0/1/2 taxonomy and that health-aggregator is the single composer. 2) Make yuri-health.mjs (ENG-LC-01) and lane-health.sh both write status files with a common envelope {tier, status, generated_at, checks[], worst_reason}. 3) health-aggregator.mjs reads both + its own launchd/hook coverage, emits .claude/state/yuri-rollup-health.json with overall = worst tier and the worst-tier reason. 4) brain-inject's laneHealth loader reads the rollup. 5) Update the graph: one health node (or a tier triad) with correct edges to brain-inject; retire the 'single dashboard' prose on yuri-health. 6) Reindex + manual + viz per continuity law. This is the L-effort capstone that ENG-LC-01 feeds into.
- **verify:** cat .claude/state/yuri-rollup-health.json shows one overall verdict with worst_reason; killing one lane flips tier-1 and the rollup worst-tier without masking the deep-probe tier; brain-inject NEURON/lane section shows the unified verdict. launchctl shows the composer scheduled; the two probe scripts feed it via status files (no double-scheduling of the same probe).

## Drift findings (5)
- yuri-circuitry-graph.json yuri-health node: calls it 'the single liveness dashboard that confirms the heavy memory and energy circuits are actually wired' — DRIFT. _SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-health.mjs has ZERO production callers (grep across _SYSTEM/Scripts, .claude/hooks, ~/Library/LaunchAgents returns only health-status.test.mjs + energy-observability-health.mjs which it itself calls), no plist, and writes NO status file (no writeFileSync — output goes to stdout and evaporates). Meanwhile TWO OTHER health monitors ARE scheduled on launchd: _SYSTEM/Scripts/lane-health.sh (com.yuri-os-musubi.lane-health.plist, writes .claude/state/lane-health-status.json) and _SYSTEM/monitoring/health-aggregator.mjs (com.yuri.health-aggregator.plist). health-aggregator.mjs:79 reads lane-health's status file but contains zero reference to yuri-health.mjs. So it is neither 'single' nor the live dashboard — it is an orphaned third harness.
- session-reflect DOUBLE-FIRE on every Stop. .claude/hooks/yuri-sentinel-stop.js:31 does require('./session-reflect.js') then reflect.run() (it is the FIRST Stop hook, settings.json:282), AND .claude/settings.json:305 fires session-reflect.js again as a standalone Stop hook. session-reflect.js:197 writeJournal prepends a fresh journal entry with no session_id idempotency guard, so the second invocation can append a duplicate journal block. The graph/description does not mention this redundancy.
- yuri-dream-processor.mjs (the dream-queue CONSUMER that routes the queued prompt to DeepSeek and appends synthesized rules to global.md) is effectively ORPHANED for autonomous operation — it appears in NO launchd plist and is not spawned by yuri-dream.js or settings.json. Only _SYSTEM/Scripts/launch-readiness-check.mjs:102 references it (an existence check, not an invocation). yuri-dream.js only QUEUES (writes dream-queue.jsonl + .dream-prompt.txt); nothing in the live wiring drains the queue on a schedule, so dream rules accumulate as 'pending' unless run manually. The yuri-dream node description ('queuing learned rules into the global/project learning ledgers') implies a closed loop that is actually open at the consumer.
- neuron-loop node files[] lists '~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.yuri-os-musubi.neuron-loop.plist' and claims 'HEALTHY, not 0-invocations' — VERIFIED accurate: synthesis.jsonl has 43 runs, last run NL-2026-06-04 ts 2026-06-04T10:00:11Z, and brain-inject.js:343 reads .claude/state/neuron-loop.log + injects the NEURON_LOOP section (brain-inject.js:433). The graph said 41 runs; live count is 43 (drift is just staleness, loop closure itself is real and correct).
- neuron-loop.mjs:277 computes externalPapers by string-replacing 'github-trending'->'arxiv-pulse' in PATHS.githubTrending and reading .papers — if knowledge-scout never writes arxiv-pulse.json this silently yields 0 with no signal that the arxiv arm is dead (masked-zero, not error). Comment at top lists 'ArXiv AI papers ingestion' as phase 4 but the read path is a fragile derived filename, not a declared PATHS entry.

### ENG-03 — Fix the lost-write race in scout-orchestrator PostToolUse + add bus file lock
`harden · effort M · risk med · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:**   ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** .claude/hooks/scout-orchestrator.js:161 · .claude/hooks/scout-orchestrator.js:168 · .claude/hooks/scout-orchestrator.js:183 · .claude/hooks/scout-bus.js:22 · .claude/hooks/scout-bus.js:27 · .claude/settings.json:244
- **why now:** Scouts are the live risk-injection organ; silently dropping a CRITICAL instant finding defeats the whole PreToolUse safety inject. Cross-refs the bug-bounty race/TOCTOU weakness class.
- **what:** Two real concurrency defects on scout-bus.json. (1) Lost write: handlePostToolUse (scout-orchestrator.js:161) calls bus.appendFinding() which internally read+writes the bus, THEN at L168 reads a STALE fresh copy into `b` and at L183 writes `b` back — clobbering the just-appended YURI-RISK-LITE finding. Fix: do the appendFinding's instant-finding write through the SAME `b` object (append into b.ring before the final writeBus(b)), or move the appendFinding call to AFTER the final read so it isn't overwritten. (2) No file lock: scout-bus.js readBus/writeBus (L22-33) use tmp+rename (atomic single-write) but assume a single writer; PostToolUse is wired `async` (settings.json:244) and PreToolUse handler also read-modify-writes (markConsumed L140) the same file — concurrent async hooks interleave and drop findings / un-consume entries. Add an advisory lock (lockfile or O_EXCL marker with stale-lock timeout + fail-OPEN to a no-op so a stuck lock never blocks a tool call).
- **agent scope:** Reproduce the lost-write first: write a tiny harness that calls handlePostToolUse with a YURI-RISK-LITE hit and asserts the instant finding survives in scout-bus.json after the handler exits (it currently does NOT). Then fix by restructuring handlePostToolUse to read `b` ONCE at the top, append the instant finding into b.ring directly (not via the self-writing appendFinding), and writeBus(b) ONCE at the end. Add an advisory lock helper in scout-bus.js (lockfile with a 5s stale timeout; on lock-acquire failure FAIL OPEN — return without writing rather than block the hook, log to scout-errors.log). Keep the tmp+rename. Do NOT touch .claude/state/ paths as protected reads — scout-bus.json lives in .claude/state/ so confirm the existing hooks already write there under owner-sanctioned hook execution (they do, via the hook runtime, not via Claude tool calls).
- **verify:** Run the repro harness: before fix it shows the YURI-RISK-LITE finding missing from the bus; after fix it survives. Spawn two concurrent handler processes against the same bus and assert no finding count regression (sum of appended == count in ring, modulo TTL). Confirm a held lock for >5s is treated as stale and the hook fails open (tool call not blocked).

A dispatch that fails at the HTTP/rail layer still emits an identical observability record as a success: the trace measures 'dispatch attempted', not 'dispatch succeeded' — mislabels failed dispatches.

Lost-write on the YURI-RISK-LITE instant finding.
- scout-orchestrator.js PostToolUse is wired async (settings.json:244-245) and PreToolUse handler also read-modify-writes the same scout-bus.json (markConsumed L140-141) — concurrent async PreToolUse + PostToolUse on the shared JSON can interleave and drop findings or un-consume entries; no atomicity beyond the tmp+rename single-writer assumption which the async fan-out violates.
