# AREA 8: Token-Efficiency & Session (circuitry layer)

**Top priority:** ENG-01 (repair the compaction-ladder feeder + unify the three token signals). The whole layer's reason for existing — automatic /compact before context death — is provably dormant: the ladder's only input (context.pct) is fed solely by the statusLine/Stop node token-status.js, which the graph doesn't even map, and a real 1081m/798-tool session logged 'peak ctx 0%, 0 compacts'. Everything else here (ledger crypto-chaining, checkpoint snapshots, cross-terminal bus) is polish on top of a primary mechanism that doesn't fire. Fix the feeder first, then the IB-compaction upgrade (Card 15) has something live to ride on.

## Improvements

### ENG-01 — Repair the compaction-ladder feeder: make context.pct fresh at PreToolUse, atomically
`drift-fix · effort M · risk med · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** read-lower-bound-not-point, gate-on-identity-not-aggregate  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** .claude/hooks/pre-tool-use.js:80 · .claude/hooks/token-status.js:71 · .claude/hooks/session-state.js:22 · .claude/skills/compact-optimizer/SKILL.md:136
- **why now:** This is the layer's primary mechanism and it is provably dormant (Session Notes 'peak ctx 0%'). Every other improvement here is downstream of the ladder actually firing.
- **what:** The 5-tier compaction ladder in pre-tool-use.js reads state.context.pct (line 81) but nothing in the Pre/PostToolUse chain writes it — the sole writer is token-status.js:writeContextPct, which runs as the statusLine command and the Stop hook. The ladder therefore decides on a stale pct (last statusLine render), and live Session Notes show 'peak ctx 0% / 0 compacts' across a 1081m session, i.e. it effectively never fires. Fix: (a) move/duplicate the pct read into a path that is guaranteed fresh — either route the real context_window.used_percentage into session-state on a hook that runs pre-decision, or have pre-tool-use.js fall back to deriving pct from the transcript token count when context.pct.last_updated is older than N seconds; (b) make ALL session-state writers (token-status.writeContextPct + ss.update) go through the single atomic session-state.js mediator so the statusLine writer and the PostToolUse writer stop clobbering each other (eliminate the second tmp+rename in token-status.js:74-80, replace with ss.update(s => { s.context.pct = pct; s.context.last_updated = ... })). Add a freshness/staleness guard so a >120s-old pct is treated as unknown and conservatively re-derived rather than trusted as 0.
- **agent scope:** 1) Add a staleness check in pre-tool-use.js before getTier(): read state.context.last_updated; if missing or older than STALE_MS (e.g. 120000) emit a one-line advisory and skip firing (do not act on a stale/zero pct). 2) Refactor token-status.js writeContextPct to call the session-state mediator's update() instead of its own read-modify-write tmp+rename, so writes are serialized through one atomic path. 3) Remove the dead _lastWrittenPct module cache (it resets every process); replace with a real throttle keyed on the persisted last_updated timestamp (skip write if <2s since last and |delta|<1). 4) Verify: simulate by writing a session-state.json with context.pct=70/last_updated=now, pipe a fake PreToolUse event into pre-tool-use.js, assert it emits a tier-2/3 hint on stderr; set last_updated to 10min ago, assert it now emits the staleness advisory and does NOT fire a compact hint.
- **verify:** node -e harness: craft .claude/state/session-state.json with context.pct=75 + fresh last_updated, echo a PreToolUse JSON into `node .claude/hooks/pre-tool-use.js`, grep stderr for 'CONTEXT' hint = fires. Re-run with last_updated set 10min in the past = staleness advisory, no compact hint. Then run two concurrent writers (token-status writeContextPct + a post-tool-use ss.update) in a loop and assert no lost updates (tools_used count + pct both survive) by diffing the final JSON against expected.

### ENG-03 — Unify or explicitly reconcile the three divergent token-fullness signals
`refactor · effort M · risk med · OWNER-GATED` · deps: 

- **patterns:** read-lower-bound-not-point  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** .claude/hooks/token-budget-check.js:14 · .claude/hooks/token-tool-logger.js:8 · .claude/hooks/token-status.js:162
- **why now:** Owner has already flagged token-ledger/token-tracking for deletion review; before deleting, the audit must show the heuristic budget signal is redundant-and-wrong vs the real one, so the owner can cut the right thing.
- **what:** There are three uncorrelated answers to 'how full / how expensive is this session': (1) context.pct (real used_percentage, drives the compaction ladder), (2) estimatedTokens (a crude heuristic sum, Agent=8000/Read=800/Edit=400, drives token-budget-check's 80k/150k WARN/CRITICAL), (3) totalTokens (real transcript input+output, drives the statusLine + weekly). token-budget-check.js:14 reads ONLY the heuristic estimatedTokens and is blind to the real numbers token-status already computes from the transcript. The heuristic can be off by 10x (it counts a Read as 800 tokens regardless of file size; a 2000-line file Read is ~30k+ real tokens). Either retire the heuristic estimator and point token-budget-check at the real token-session.json totals, or keep the heuristic ONLY as a pre-first-statusLine cold-start bootstrap and switch to real numbers as soon as token-status has written totalTokens once. Document which signal is authoritative for which decision.
- **agent scope:** Decide authority: real transcript tokens (token-session.json totalTokens) are ground truth once available. Rewrite token-budget-check.js to (a) prefer token-session.json totalTokens if updatedAt is recent, (b) fall back to /tmp estimatedTokens only when no real number exists yet. Keep the 80k/150k thresholds but re-validate them against real cumulative tokens (note: 'context.pct' and 'cumulative tokens' are different axes — a budget check on cumulative spend is legitimate and distinct from the context-window ladder, so KEEP both but stop pretending the heuristic estimate approximates either). Add a one-line comment block at the top of each of the 3 files stating which signal it owns and which it must not touch.
- **verify:** Write a token-session.json with totalTokens=120000/updatedAt=now and an empty /tmp estimatedTokens; pipe a PreToolUse event into token-budget-check.js; assert it emits the 🟠 WARN (>=80k) off the REAL number, not silence. Set updatedAt 1h old + estimatedTokens=200000; assert defined fallback behavior. Confirm token-status totalTokens and token-budget-check now reference the same file.

### ENG-04 — Wire Card 15 (Information Bottleneck) into the compact hint builder
`math-transfer · effort L · risk med · OWNER-GATED` · deps: ENG-01

- **patterns:** read-lower-bound-not-point, replace-hand-tuned-constant, compose-readonly-analyzer  ·  **transfers:** Card 15 — Information Bottleneck → relevance-preserving compaction
- **files:** .claude/hooks/pre-tool-use.js:29 · .claude/skills/compact-optimizer/SKILL.md:51 · 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/math-theory-transfer-catalog-2026-06-03.md · _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/math-kernel.mjs
- **why now:** Card 15's named YURI target IS this organ's compact hint; it is the single ready-to-wire Tier-2 transfer for this layer and only becomes useful once ENG-01 makes the ladder fire.
- **what:** The /compact hint is currently built by pre-tool-use.js:buildHint() as a fixed string (branch + last 3 files + last aversion) and by the compact-optimizer skill as a hand-ranked Survival Priority Hierarchy. Card 15 (Information Bottleneck → relevance-preserving compaction) is the exact Tier-2 math engine for this organ: score each survival candidate by ibScore(chunk) = relevanceToObjective(chunk, goalSpine+openClaims) − β·tokenCost(chunk), with the CRITICAL fix that relevance must be MAX-POOL over live objectives (max_i overlap(chunk, goal_i + open_claims_i)), not a union/sum, so Marcel's parallel-branch style doesn't starve narrow-but-critical facts. β is the single honest tunable, exposed in the cockpit. Build it as a read-only analyzer that ranks candidate survival chunks (files_written, aversions, last error, plan steps, open claims) and emits the top-K into the hint, with an empty-objective graceful-degenerate guardrail that equals today's self-information baseline. Relevance scored via the existing FTS5/BM25 corpus index (ai search). Ship advisory; never auto-drop operator-pinned context.
- **agent scope:** 1) Add an ibScore() to a new tier-2 module (e.g. _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/compaction-ib.mjs) exporting rankSurvivalChunks(candidates, objectives, beta). relevance = max over objectives of BM25/overlap(chunk, objective_text); tokenCost = estimateTokensFromText (reuse token-ledger.mjs estimateTokensFromText). 2) Candidates = {files_written entries, aversions, last error, plan_dispatch_gate steps, open claims from the claim cortex if available}. 3) Guardrail: if objectives is empty, return the current self-information ordering (today's buildHint) unchanged. 4) β read from energy-weights.json (the one honest dial) with a sane default (e.g. 0.5). 5) buildHint() calls rankSurvivalChunks and emits top-K into the existing /compact Preserve string; NEVER drop an operator-pinned/aversion chunk regardless of score. 6) Mirror the same ibScore as a salience re-gate hook is OUT of scope here — compaction only.
- **verify:** Unit: feed a synthetic transcript with one narrow-but-critical fact tied to goal_2 and lots of noise tied to goal_1; assert max-pool keeps the narrow fact while a sum/union baseline drops it (A/B the two aggregators, assert different survivors). Guardrail test: objectives=[] → output byte-identical to current buildHint(). β sweep: assert higher β shortens the hint monotonically. Run on a real long transcript and eyeball relevance/size curve per the card's smallest-experiment.

`harden · effort M · risk med · auto` · deps:

Pick A if owner wants minimal blast radius, B if the telemetry is needed in full.
- **verify:** Simulate 1000 ss.update calls in a loop; assert session-state.json stays under a size ceiling (e.g. <64KB) and array lengths are capped. ### ENG-10 — Cross-terminal memory bus: replace ps-walk session-id with a stable env-anchored id
`harden · effort M · risk med · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** gate-on-identity-not-aggregate  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** .claude/hooks/memory-bus.js:19 · .claude/hooks/pre-tool-use.js:53 · .claude/hooks/post-tool-use.js:58
- **why now:** Cross-terminal awareness is the node's whole purpose; a ps-name-gated key is exactly the kind of launch-shape-fragile assumption that breaks silently on the tmux/PTY launch shape YURI mandates.
- **what:** memory-bus.js getSessionId() (lines 19-44) derives the cross-terminal session key by shelling out to `ps -p` twice to walk the process tree (Claude→sh→node), parsing comm/ppid. This is fragile: it depends on the parent being named /^(node|claude)/, breaks under different launch shapes (tmux/PTY, wrappers, future Claude binaries not named 'node'/'claude'), and runs two execSync calls on the PreToolUse AND PostToolUse hot path. The cross-terminal feature (one terminal seeing another's memory writes) silently no-ops or mis-keys if the ps-walk guesses wrong. Anchor the session id to a stable env var when available (CLAUDE_SESSION_ID) and fall back to the ps-walk only when absent; cache the resolved id in a module/temp marker so the double-execSync doesn't run on every tool call.

- **verify:** Set CLAUDE_SESSION_ID=A, call getSessionId() → 'claude_A' (or stable A-derived); set =B → distinct. Unset env, assert ps-walk fallback still returns a value. Time getSessionId() x1000 with cache vs without; assert no double-execSync on cached path. Existing memory-bus tests (.claude/hooks/tests/) still pass.

## Drift findings (11)
- context.pct — the entire 5-tier compaction ladder's only input — is written EXCLUSIVELY by token-status.js (.claude/hooks/token-status.js:71-82 writeContextPct), which is the statusLine command + Stop hook, NOT a PreToolUse hook. pre-tool-use.js:81 reads context.pct on every PreToolUse but nothing in the PreToolUse/PostToolUse chain ever refreshes it. The ladder runs on whatever pct the last statusLine render left behind. Circuitry node 'pre-tool-use' description ('Reads session-state.json context.pct ... emits /compact hints on a 5-tier ladder') omits that its sole feeder is a different node entirely (token-status) that is not in the graph at all.
- token-status.js is a LIVE, load-bearing node (the statusLine command in settings.json statusLine.command + a Stop hook) but is ABSENT from the circuitry graph's Token-Efficiency & Session layer. It is the single writer of context.pct AND the single writer of real per-session transcript token counts (token-session.json). A core organ is unmapped. (.claude/settings.json statusLine + Stop block; .claude/hooks/token-status.js:71,113,203)
- THREE divergent, never-reconciled 'how full are we' signals: (1) context.pct from real context_window.used_percentage via token-status (drives the compaction ladder), (2) estimatedTokens heuristic sum in /tmp/claude-session-*.json (Agent=8000/Read=800/Edit=400, token-tool-logger.js:8-12,29) drives token-budget-check's 80k/150k WARN/CRITICAL, (3) totalTokens real transcript tokens in token-session.json (token-status.js:162) drives the statusLine display + weekly. token-budget-check.js:14 reads estimatedTokens and NEVER sees the real transcript numbers; the two systems can disagree by an order of magnitude and never cross-check.
- _lastWrittenPct module-level cache (token-status.js:69-73) with the `< 1` dedup is dead code across invocations — the statusLine spawns a fresh node process each render, so _lastWrittenPct resets to -1 every time and the dedup guard never actually suppresses a write. The intended write-throttling does not exist.
- Two non-coordinated writers race on session-state.json: session-state.js write() uses atomic tmp+rename (line 16-20) and is used by post-tool-use ss.update; token-status.js writeContextPct (line 74-80) does its OWN read-modify-write with a separate tmp path. A PostToolUse ss.update and a concurrent statusLine writeContextPct can clobber each other (last-writer-wins on the whole file), silently dropping a tools_used/files_written entry or a pct update.
- compact-optimizer Session Notes record 'peak ctx: 0% | compacts: 0' for a 1081-minute / 798-Bash-call session (.claude/skills/compact-optimizer/SKILL.md:136-137) — direct evidence the pct pipeline reported 0% through a massive session and the ladder never fired. Either statusLine isn't feeding pct in that run-shape or session-reflect reads a stale/zeroed field. The ladder is dormant in practice, not just in theory.
- compact_history is an unbounded array (pre-tool-use.js:96,101,117,128 all .push, no cap) living inside session-state.json which is re-read+rewritten in full on every PostToolUse ss.update and every statusLine writeContextPct. On a long session this grows the hot-path JSON file monotonically — O(n) serialize cost per tool call.
- post-tool-use.js (the 'main bookkeeper' for Token-Efficiency & Session) writes design-memory.json (lines 62-87) — a design-master organ — inline in the session-bookkeeper hot path. Wrong-layer coupling: a design concern embedded in the token/session bookkeeper, re-reading+rewriting a 100-entry JSON on every .tsx/.css/.html/.scss write.
- token-tool-logger keys its session off /tmp/claude-current-session (token-tool-logger.js:22), which token-session-init.js:19 OVERWRITES on every SessionStart including subagent starts. A subagent SessionStart repoints /tmp/claude-current-session to a fresh empty session file, so the root session's estimatedTokens accounting is silently reset/forked mid-session — the budget signal that token-budget-check depends on is corruptible by any subagent boot.
- compact-optimizer SKILL.md says trigger /compact at '65%+ context OR 40k transcript' (lines 87,109) but the live ladder in pre-tool-use.js getTier() fires tier-2 auto-compact at pct<73 tokenmaxxing / <78 normal — the skill's documented 65% threshold and the executable ladder's thresholds disagree, and neither references the 40k-transcript trigger anywhere in code (no transcript-line counter exists in the hooks).
- token-ledger pricing is stale vs the running model: token-ledger.mjs:65-67 prices 'claude-opus-4-7' and 'claude-sonnet-4-6'; this session runs Opus 4.8 (claude-opus-4-8). findModelPricing (line 775) substring-matches, so 'claude-opus-4-8' matches NO key and silently falls through to pricing.default = all-zeros — every Opus-4.8 ledger event records cost_usd=0.

# AREA 9: Hidden / Meta / Self-referential layer (boot machinery, per-turn re-inject, soft-compliance gates, shared protected-path/identity libs)

**Top priority:** HID-01 (excise the dead pulse-orchestrator/pulse-packager path from user-prompt-submit.js) is the single highest-leverage item. It is in the hottest self-referential hook (fires every turn under a <50ms budget), the graph already declares the path 'retired in code' so the code currently lies-by-omission, and the dead ORCHESTRATOR const points at a non-existent file (Scripts/ vs _SYSTEM/Scripts/ — verified by ls) while the real 30KB orphan sits unreferenced. Removing it both honors the continuity law (graph must match code) and unblocks HID-02 (retire the orphans) and HID-08 (fix the design-context stall in the same file). Tightly scoped, locally verifiable, and it cleans the most-executed organ in the layer.

### HID-05 — Make musubi-protocol-enforce thresholds data-driven (replace-hand-tuned-constant) and fix the throttle-eats-violations bug
`math-transfer · effort M · risk low · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** replace-hand-tuned-constant, read-lower-bound-not-point  ·  **transfers:** delta-gate-severity-laundering, neuro-tunables-map
- **files:** .claude/hooks/musubi-protocol-enforce.js:11 · .claude/hooks/musubi-protocol-enforce.js:37 · .claude/hooks/musubi-protocol-enforce.js:79-94
- **why now:** It is the layer's only soft-compliance organ and currently can silently fail to fire during the exact spree it exists to catch; plus it carries the textbook hand-tuned-constant the propagation taxonomy wants externalized.
- **what:** musubi-protocol-enforce.js has two issues. (1) Hand-tuned magic constants: ENFORCE_THROTTLE_MS=60000 (:11) and the directWrites>3 threshold (:37) are inline literals — exactly the replace-hand-tuned-constant mechanismPattern. (2) Throttle-eats-violations bug: the hook updates aeonic.lastEnforceAt BEFORE checking violations isn't the issue — the real bug is it updates lastEnforceAt unconditionally at :92-94 even when no violation fires, AND it bails early at :79-81 when throttled. So if writes 4,5,6 happen inside one 60s window, the FIRST substantive tool resets the throttle and the actual >3 condition (which needs the writes to accumulate) can be permanently skipped — the advisory may never surface during a genuine direct-write spree. The threshold check reads state.tools_used (populated by post-tool-use.js:26, verified), so the data is real; the throttle logic just races it. Transfer: externalize threshold+window to _SYSTEM/SELF/energy-weights.json (the canonical neuro-knob surface per neuro-tunables-map) or a small enforce-config, and only advance lastEnforceAt when a violation actually fired (so a clean window doesn't suppress the next dirty one).
- **agent scope:** In musubi-protocol-enforce.js: (1) lift ENFORCE_THROTTLE_MS and the directWrites threshold (currently >3) into a config read once at startup — prefer _SYSTEM/SELF/energy-weights.json under a new 'aeonicEnforce' key {throttleMs, maxDirectWritesBeforeAdvisory} with the current values as defaults; fall back to literals if the file/key is absent (fail-open, never block boot). (2) Fix the throttle race: only set state.aeonic.lastEnforceAt = now AFTER violations.length>0 (move the write from :92-94 into the violations-emitted branch), so a clean substantive tool inside the window doesn't reset the clock and swallow a real spree. Keep advisory-only (never blocks). Add a test: simulate state with tools_used=[Write,Write,Write,Write] and lastEnforceAt=0 -> expect the offload_default advisory; then a second call 1s later with the same state -> assert it now correctly throttles (no double-fire) but the FIRST one fired. Negative: tools_used=[Write,Write] -> no advisory.
- **verify:** Unit test: feed crafted session-state via the injected stateModule (or a temp STATE_FILE) — 4 writes/0 agents emits offload_default once; 2 writes emits nothing; confirm lastEnforceAt only advances on a real violation. Tunable surfaces in energy-weights.json and changing it changes behavior without editing the hook.

### HID-08 — Resolve the design-context-inject double-trigger between DESIGN_PATTERN and skill-auto-trigger in user-prompt-submit
`refactor · effort M · risk med · auto` · deps: HID-01

- **patterns:** compose-readonly-analyzer, read-lower-bound-not-point  ·  **transfers:** harness-batch-and-headless
- **files:** .claude/hooks/user-prompt-submit.js:180 · .claude/hooks/user-prompt-submit.js:258-271
- **why now:** A hook with an explicit <50ms exit contract is calling execSync with a 5s timeout on a broad regex — every design-adjacent prompt eats that stall. It's the clearest correctness/perf drift in the live per-turn path.
- **what:** user-prompt-submit.js fires design context two ways that overlap: (1) buildSkillAutoTrigger (:170) matches '/design','design this',etc -> emits an <auto-skill-trigger> pointing to design-master; (2) the separate DESIGN_PATTERN regex (:180, far broader — matches 'svg','color','font','theme','animation','dashboard'...) -> shells out to design-context-inject.mjs synchronously (execSync, 5s timeout, :261) and injects a <design-master-context> block. On a prompt like 'change the dashboard font color' BOTH fire: the skill hint AND a 5s-budget synchronous subprocess on the per-turn hook that's supposed to exit <50ms. The execSync at :261 directly violates the hook's own <50ms exit guarantee (comment :11) — a slow design-context-inject stalls every design-adjacent prompt. Also the broad DESIGN_PATTERN will false-positive on non-design prompts mentioning 'animation' or 'theme' in a code context. Fix: make the design-context inject async/detached (write to a sentinel the NEXT turn consumes, mirroring the L5b recall pattern already in this file) OR gate it behind a tighter signal, and de-dupe so a single design intent doesn't both hint the skill and inject the heavy context.
- **agent scope:** In user-prompt-submit.js: (1) the execSync design-context-inject (:259-267) breaks the <50ms budget — convert to the same detached prior-turn-lag pattern as L5b recall: spawn design-context-inject.mjs detached, write its output to a .claude/state sentinel, and consume the PRIOR turn's design-context envelope at the top of this turn (consume-once unlink). (2) Tighten or remove the redundancy: if buildSkillAutoTrigger already matched design-master for this prompt, the design-context block is the useful payload — keep it; but narrow DESIGN_PATTERN so it doesn't fire on incidental 'theme/animation/color' tokens in non-design code prompts (require a design verb OR a design noun+'design'/'ui'/'style' co-occurrence). Add a test: 'fix the animation timing bug in physics.js' should NOT trigger design-context; 'redesign the dashboard' SHOULD. Assert the hook exits well under budget (no synchronous 5s subprocess) by timing a design-prompt invocation.
- **verify:** Time the hook on a design prompt: node user-prompt-submit.js <<< design-payload completes in <100ms (no synchronous design-context-inject subprocess in-band); 'fix the animation bug in foo.js' produces no <design-master-context> block; 'redesign the hud' produces the design path. grep execSync user-prompt-submit.js returns 0 (converted to detached spawn).

# AREA 10: Cross-Reference Engine seam — axes 1 (unify-surfaces), 3 (drift-detection / continuity-law mechanization), 4 (confidence + provenance). Axis 2 (mechanism-signatures) already shaped in three-seams-shaped-with-prior-art-2026-06-04.md; this spec makes 1/3/4 coherent with it.

**Top priority:** XREF-03 (the drift-detector / continuity-law mechanizer) — it converts an unenforced memory rule into executable enforcement, is the read-only safety precondition for honest provenance (axis 4) and the offload->cross-ref slot rename, and is literally the mechanized form of the manual audit this session just ran (which caught 1 phantom, a 23-commit-stale gitnexus index, and 3 stale doc-counts).

## Improvements

### XREF-01 — Unified retrieval surface — one `ai xref` query across FTS5 + circuitry-graph + GitNexus + spectrum, merged + provenance-tagged
`new-mechanism · effort L · risk low · auto` · deps: XREF-04

- **patterns:** compose-readonly-analyzer  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/xref-query.mjs:NEW · _SYSTEM/Scripts/ai:961 (add `xref` case next to search|find) · _SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-search.mjs:27 (import buildMatch, do not duplicate) · 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/yuri-circuitry-graph.json (read-only graph surface) · 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/yuri-mechanism-spectrum-267-2026-06-03.md (spectrum grep surface)
- **why now:** The meta-law (hold breadth+depth, cross-reference everything) has no executable surface today — four siloed retrieval tools and a human stitching them. This is the 'unify the surfaces' axis literally. It is also the read-path the propagation/cross-ref engine will call, so building the merged query first means propagation-scan consumes a clean API instead of re-stitching surfaces.
- **what:** Axis 1 (unify-surfaces). Today the four retrieval surfaces are siloed: FTS5 corpus search lives behind `ai search` -> yuri-search.mjs (38,742 docs, bm25); the circuitry-graph is a raw JSON file read ad-hoc; GitNexus is MCP-only (query/context/impact) and 23 commits stale; the mechanism-spectrum (267 mechanisms) is a flat markdown doc with no query surface. There is NO single command that asks one question across all four and returns a MERGED, deduped, provenance-tagged result set. Build `_SYSTEM/Scripts/xref-query.mjs` exposed as `ai xref "<query>" [--node <id>] [--top N] [--json]` wired into the `ai` bash dispatcher (new case alongside search|find at _SYSTEM/Scripts/ai:961). It runs four bounded passes: (1) FTS5 — reuse yuri-search.mjs buildMatch()+the existing search-index.db (do NOT reimplement the injection-hardened matcher; import it); (2) GRAPH — load yuri-circuitry-graph.json, lexical-match query tokens against node description+triggeredBy+label, AND if --node given pull 1-hop neighbors by edge kind; (3) GITNEXUS — shell to the MCP-equivalent or `npx gitnexus` query for structural hits (gated: if index stale per XREF-03's check, tag every gitnexus hit `provenance.stale=true` and downrank); (4) SPECTRUM — grep the 267-mechanism doc for layer/mechanism-name hits. Merge into one ranked list where EACH hit carries {surface, path, snippet, score, provenance{kind, confidence, stale?}} per the axis-4 schema (XREF-04). Dedup by path (a file hit in both FTS5 and gitnexus collapses to one row, provenance.surfaces=[fts5,gitnexus], confidence=max). Bounded output (default top 10, hard cap 50, mirrors yuri-search). This is the 'one query across surfaces' the meta-law needs — the live face of holding breadth. It does NOT auto-fire and does NOT mutate; pure read front-door.
- **agent scope:** Create _SYSTEM/Scripts/xref-query.mjs (Node ESM, better-sqlite3 readonly like yuri-search.mjs). Export `xrefQuery(rawQuery, {node, top, json})` returning {ok, query, merged:[{surface,path,snippet,score,provenance}], counts:{fts5,graph,gitnexus,spectrum,merged,deduped}}. Pass 1: import buildMatch from yuri-search.mjs and run the SAME bm25 query against _SYSTEM/OS_KERNEL/search-index.db readonly. Pass 2: JSON.parse the circuitry-graph, tokenize query, score each node by token overlap on (description+triggeredBy+label), and if `node` id given add its 1-hop neighbors tagged with edge.kind. Pass 3: gitnexus — invoke via child_process `npx gitnexus` query OR document the MCP call; wrap in try/catch, on failure emit zero gitnexus hits with a `gitnexusUnavailable:true` flag (fail-soft, never throw). Pass 4: read the spectrum md, grep query tokens against layer headers + mechanism names. Merge: build a Map keyed by normalized path; on collision keep max score, union provenance.surfaces, set confidence per XREF-04 rules. Sort by score, slice to top. Wire `xref` into _SYSTEM/Scripts/ai at the search|find case (line ~961): `exec node "$SCRIPT_DIR/xref-query.mjs" "$@"`. Add usage text. DO NOT auto-inject, DO NOT write any file, DO NOT touch protected paths. Bounded output cap 50.
- **verify:** Run `_SYSTEM/Scripts/ai xref "energy gate protected path veto" --json` and confirm: (a) merged array has hits from >=2 surfaces with distinct provenance.surface tags; (b) a path appearing in both FTS5 and graph appears ONCE with provenance.surfaces=[fts5,graph]; (c) counts.deduped > 0 when overlap exists; (d) with gitnexus index stale, every gitnexus hit carries provenance.stale=true. Negative: `ai xref ""` returns ok:false empty-query (mirror yuri-search.mjs:58). Confirm no file under _SYSTEM/state or backend/data was written (git status clean except the new script).

### XREF-03 — Drift-detector — mechanize the continuity law: auto-verify every circuitry seam still exists at path:line
`new-mechanism · effort M · risk low · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** compose-readonly-analyzer  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/xref-drift-scan.mjs:NEW · 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/yuri-circuitry-graph.json (read-only) · _SYSTEM/Scripts/ai (optional: add `xref-drift` case) · _SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-closeout.mjs (optional EOT wiring — owner-gated)
- **why now:** The continuity law is currently unenforced trust. This session's manual audit IS the drift-detector run by hand — mechanizing it means the graph stops rotting silently. It is also the safety precondition for the offload->cross-ref slot rename (roadmap §10.2): the continuity law forbids a false graph rename, and you can only prove the rename is honest if a drift-scan confirms the new seam exists where the graph claims.
- **what:** Axis 3 (live-verification / drift-detection). The continuity law ([[circuitry-change-propagation-continuity]]) says any change must propagate to graph+viz+manual+reverify+reindex — but it is a MEMORY RULE with ZERO executable enforcement (verified: only energy-weights-drift.test.mjs exists, and it tests config equality, not seam existence). The graph can silently rot against live code; this session manually found 1 phantom node, a 23-commit-stale gitnexus index, and 3 stale doc-counts — exactly the rot a drift-detector should catch. Build `_SYSTEM/Scripts/xref-drift-scan.mjs` (read-only, manual CLI + optional later hook). For each of the 83 circuitry nodes it checks: (a) FILE EXISTENCE — every path in node.files exists on disk (catches phantoms / moved files; would have flagged cross-domain-transfer-engine which is intentionally files:[] -> allow an explicit `phantomOk:true` allowlist so intentional phantoms don't false-alarm); (b) GITNEXUS STALENESS — compare gitnexus list_repos indexedAt-commit vs `git rev-parse HEAD`; if behind, emit a single WARN `gitnexus-stale N commits` (this is the structural-signature substrate; a stale index = low-confidence structural hits); (c) DESCRIPTION-CLAIM DRIFT (bounded) — for nodes whose description asserts a concrete count or a 'LIVE/UNWIRED/PHANTOM' status, re-derive what is cheaply checkable (e.g. file present? grep for the claimed trigger string in the file?) and flag mismatches as advisory. Output a deterministic report: per-node PASS / DRIFT lines using the Evidence Contract grammar (FILE_COUNT / MATCH). This is the continuity law made into a standing check — it does not fix drift, it SURFACES it, fail-LOUD. Run it in EOT closeout and optionally as a PostToolUse-on-graph-edit advisory (see XREF-03b).
- **agent scope:** Create _SYSTEM/Scripts/xref-drift-scan.mjs (Node ESM, read-only). Load yuri-circuitry-graph.json. For each node: (1) for each f in node.files, fs.existsSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT,f)) — collect missing; honor a `PHANTOM_OK = new Set(['cross-domain-transfer-engine'])` allowlist so the one intentional phantom is not flagged (and ASSERT it stays files:[] — if an intentional phantom suddenly has files, that is itself drift). (2) Git/GitNexus staleness: `git rev-parse HEAD` vs the gitnexus-indexed commit (read from list_repos output or a cached marker); emit `GITNEXUS_STALE commits=N` if HEAD is ahead. (3) Bounded claim re-derivation: if node.triggeredBy or description contains a quoted file/symbol/trigger string and node.files is non-empty, grep that file for the string; emit MATCH or DRIFT. Output Evidence Contract lines: `FILE_COUNT file=<path> count=<0|1>`, `MATCH file=<path> term=<trigger> line=<N>`, and a final `XREF_DRIFT summary nodes=83 pass=K drift=M`. JSON mode for machine use. Exit code 0 always (advisory) unless `--strict` is passed (then nonzero on any DRIFT, for CI/EOT gating). NEVER write, NEVER touch protected paths, NEVER auto-reindex (just report the staleness). Add a `xref-drift` case to the `ai` dispatcher.
- **verify:** Run `node _SYSTEM/Scripts/xref-drift-scan.mjs --json` on current HEAD and confirm: (a) it reports cross-domain-transfer-engine as phantom-OK (allowlisted, not a DRIFT); (b) it reports GITNEXUS_STALE with the real 23-commit delta (HEAD 29c49062 vs indexed 712c760e); (c) all 82 file-bearing nodes resolve to existing files (pass) OR it correctly names any that moved. Negative test: temporarily point one node's files[] at a nonexistent path in a scratch copy of the graph and confirm it flags FILE_COUNT count=0 DRIFT. Confirm exit 0 without --strict, nonzero with --strict when drift present.

### XREF-03b — Continuity-law trigger — PostToolUse advisory when the circuitry graph or a node's source file is edited without a drift re-scan
`wiring · effort M · risk med · OWNER-GATED` · deps: XREF-03

- **patterns:** compose-readonly-analyzer  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** .claude/hooks/continuity-law-advisory.mjs:NEW · .claude/settings.json (register PostToolUse matcher=Edit|Write, async) · _SYSTEM/Scripts/xref-build-fileindex.mjs:NEW (bakes file->node reverse index) · 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/circuitry/file-node-index.json:NEW (baked artifact)
- **why now:** Without an automated trigger the drift-scan only runs when someone remembers — which is exactly the failure mode the continuity law exists to prevent. Owner-gated + flag-defaulted-OFF because a PostToolUse hook on every Edit/Write needs noise-tuning before it is always-on; ship it dormant-armed.
- **what:** Axis 3, the AUTOMATION leg. XREF-03 is the checker; this wires WHEN it fires. The continuity law says 'any change -> propagate'. Mechanize the trigger as a lightweight PostToolUse advisory (NOT a blocking gate — fail-open advisory, consistent with the gitnexus-hook precedent at ~/.claude/hooks/gitnexus/gitnexus-hook.cjs which is advisory enrichment under 10ms). When an Edit/Write touches either (a) yuri-circuitry-graph.json itself, or (b) a file that is listed in some node's files[] array, emit a one-line advisory: `continuity-law: <file> is circuitry node <id> — run `ai xref-drift` + propagate to viz/manual/reindex`. This is the standing daemon the seam asks about — answered as a hook, NOT a daemon (a 60s-poll daemon is wasteful for an event that only matters on graph-relevant edits; the PostToolUse path is the correct event source, same pattern as energy-tick at settings.json L259). Build a baked reverse-index (file-path -> node-id) at graph-build time so the hook is an O(1) Map lookup, never a full graph scan per tool call.
- **agent scope:** Step 1: build `_SYSTEM/Scripts/xref-build-fileindex.mjs` that reads yuri-circuitry-graph.json and writes 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/circuitry/file-node-index.json = {normalizedFilePath: nodeId}. Step 2: build `.claude/hooks/continuity-law-advisory.mjs` (PostToolUse). Read the tool input file_path; normalize relative to REPO_ROOT; O(1) lookup in the baked index; ALSO match if file_path === the graph json itself. If hit, print a single advisory line to the hook's additionalContext (NOT a block — return success, this is fail-open advisory). Hard timeout budget ~10ms (mirror gitnexus-hook). Step 3: register in .claude/settings.json PostToolUse with matcher Edit|Write, async, behind an env flag (YURI_CONTINUITY_ADVISORY=1) defaulted OFF for first ship (owner arms it after observing noise). Do NOT make it blocking. Do NOT scan the graph per-call (use the baked index). Regenerate the index as part of the graph-build/propagation continuity step.
- **verify:** After building, Edit a file known to be in a node's files[] (e.g. _SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-search.mjs, node yuri-search) with the flag on, and confirm the advisory line names node=yuri-search. Edit a file NOT in any node and confirm SILENCE (no false advisory). Confirm the hook adds <15ms to the tool call (time it) and never blocks (tool still succeeds). Confirm with flag OFF the hook is a no-op. Negative: stale index (node file moved) should degrade gracefully, not crash the hook.

### XREF-04 — Confidence + provenance schema — every cross-ref hit carries graded confidence by surface, with mandatory mismatch for low-confidence
`new-mechanism · effort M · risk low · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** gate-on-identity-not-aggregate, compose-readonly-analyzer  ·  **transfers:** three-seams SEAM-1 (Semgrep negative-fixture); roadmap §4 mandatory-mismatch containing-gate
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/xref-provenance.mjs:NEW (shared schema + confidence scorer) · _SYSTEM/config/schemas/xref-hit.schema.json:NEW · _SYSTEM/Scripts/xref-query.mjs (imports the scorer) · 02_RESOURCES/RESEARCH/yuri-circuitry-graph.json (edge.kind is the MED-tier input)
- **why now:** Provenance+confidence is the structural defense against the #1 failure mode (mechanism-fit theater). It must be a SHARED module so XREF-01 and propagation-scan grade hits identically — building it as a standalone first prevents the two organs from each inventing their own (divergent, untrustworthy) confidence model. It is also the precondition for honest axis-1 merge (a merged hit needs a confidence to rank by).
- **what:** Axis 4 (confidence + provenance per hit). The roadmap's sharpest named risk is 'mechanism-fit theater' — lexical overlap surfacing vocabulary-twins not mechanism-twins until the queue floods and trust dies. The containing gate is a graded confidence model where provenance DETERMINES confidence and low-confidence hits carry a MANDATORY human-readable mismatch field (or are auto-suppressed). Define the canonical hit schema as a shared module `_SYSTEM/Scripts/xref-provenance.mjs` that XREF-01 (query) and the future propagation-scan both import — single source so the two organs cannot diverge. Schema per hit: {path, surface, snippet, score, provenance:{evidenceKind, confidence:0..1, stale?:bool, mismatch?:string}}. CONFIDENCE RULES grounded in the verified surface reality: (1) GITNEXUS structural call-graph hit (shared calls/reads edge-kind, same math-kernel primitive imported, same before-shape) = HIGH (0.8-1.0) — BUT multiply by a staleness penalty if XREF-03 reports the index behind HEAD (a structural claim on a 23-commit-stale graph is NOT high-confidence); (2) GRAPH same-layer/1-hop neighbor via edge.kind in {calls,reads} = MED (0.55-0.75) — EXCLUDE `writes` edges from sibling-confidence (verified data-flow != shared mechanism, three-seams doc + edge-kind census calls:77/reads:31/writes:45); (3) FTS5/lexical-only hit with no structural corroboration = LOW (<0.55) and the `mismatch` field becomes MANDATORY — the author/engine must name the specific shared mechanism or the hit is auto-routed to a low-confidence sub-log, never the main surface. This is the Semgrep negative-fixture discipline (three-seams SEAM-1) applied at QUERY time: a low-confidence hit that cannot name why-it-is-a-twin is suppressed, killing theater before it floods.
- **agent scope:** Create _SYSTEM/Scripts/xref-provenance.mjs exporting: (a) `scoreHit({surface, structuralMatch, edgeKind, lexicalScore, gitnexusStale})` -> {evidenceKind, confidence, stale} using the tiered rules: gitnexus-structural -> 0.8-1.0 (multiply by 0.6 if gitnexusStale); graph-neighbor on calls|reads -> 0.55-0.75; graph-neighbor on writes -> EXCLUDED (return null, do not surface as sibling); lexical-only -> <0.55 with requireMismatch:true. (b) `gateHit(hit)` -> if confidence < 0.55 AND no hit.provenance.mismatch string, return {surfaced:false, sublog:true} (suppress to low-confidence sub-log); else {surfaced:true}. (c) a JSON schema at _SYSTEM/config/schemas/xref-hit.schema.json with additionalProperties:false (closed schema, fail-closed on unknown fields — consistent with the privacy-gate ALLOWED_STRING_PATHS discipline). Make XREF-01's xref-query.mjs import scoreHit/gateHit so every merged hit is graded and theater-gated identically. Document the constants as a tunable neuro-knob block (the 0.55 floor, the 0.6 staleness penalty) so they can be tuned, not hardcoded magic. NEVER surface a writes-edge sibling as a mechanism twin. NEVER surface a sub-0.55 hit without a named mismatch.
- **verify:** Unit-test scoreHit: a gitnexus structural hit with gitnexusStale=true must score BELOW the same hit with stale=false (penalty applies). A writes-edge neighbor must return null (not surfaced). A lexical-only hit with no mismatch must gateHit -> surfaced:false. Validate a hit object against xref-hit.schema.json and confirm an unknown field is REJECTED (closed schema). Integration: run XREF-01 `ai xref` and confirm every merged hit has a provenance.evidenceKind + numeric confidence, and that no hit on a `writes` edge appears as a sibling.

### XREF-05 — Fail-closed hardening for the merged query — gitnexus-unavailable and stale-index must NOT silently degrade to lexical-only high-confidence
`harden · effort S · risk low · auto` · deps: XREF-01, XREF-04

- **patterns:** gate-on-identity-not-aggregate  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/xref-query.mjs (fail-closed branch + repo pinning) · _SYSTEM/Scripts/xref-provenance.mjs (structuralUnavailable downgrade)
- **why now:** Security gates fail CLOSED on bad input (FB:GATE-HARDENING-FAIL-CLOSED). A cross-ref engine that silently degrades structural->lexical while keeping the high-confidence presentation is the exact trust-killer the whole provenance axis exists to prevent. Cheap to add at build time, expensive to retrofit after the queue is trusted.
- **what:** Cross-cutting hardening across axes 1/3/4. The dangerous failure: XREF-01's gitnexus pass throws or returns nothing (stale, wrong-repo, MCP down) and the engine silently falls back to lexical-only hits but still presents them as if structurally corroborated — laundering a LOW hit into a perceived HIGH one (this is structurally the same severity-laundering class as the delta-gate finding in memory FB:DELTA-GATE-SEVERITY-LAUNDERING). Fail-CLOSED: when the structural leg is unavailable, every hit that would have needed structural corroboration is explicitly DOWNGRADED and tagged `provenance.structuralUnavailable:true`, never silently promoted. Also harden the two verified gitnexus footguns: (a) pin the `repo` param to the LIVE root path — there are TWO indexed yuri-os repos and an unpinned query can hit the stale worktree at .claude/worktrees/vault-restructure; (b) treat embeddings=0 as a known constraint (keyword-only ranking) and do not claim 'semantic' matching the index cannot deliver. Add a serialize-then-revalidate canary on the merged-hit output (mirror the energy-trace privacy-gate canary) so a malformed provenance object cannot ship.
- **agent scope:** In xref-query.mjs: wrap the gitnexus pass; on ANY failure/empty/stale set a session flag structuralLegAvailable=false. In xref-provenance.mjs scoreHit: when structuralLegAvailable=false, any hit that would have been HIGH (structural) is capped at the lexical-only ceiling (<0.55) and tagged provenance.structuralUnavailable:true — NEVER presented as structurally-corroborated. Pin gitnexus repo param to the absolute live root (reject/ignore the .claude/worktrees/vault-restructure repo). Do not use the word 'semantic' in output (embeddings=0). Add a final canary: JSON.stringify(merged) then JSON.parse and re-validate against xref-hit.schema.json before returning; on mismatch throw (fail-closed). Document each as a named footgun in a header comment.
- **verify:** Simulate gitnexus-down (rename/block the binary or force the pass to throw) and confirm: NO hit reports confidence >= 0.55 as structurally-corroborated; every affected hit carries structuralUnavailable:true. Confirm the query never silently targets the worktree repo (log which repo path was used). Confirm the serialize-revalidate canary rejects a deliberately-malformed provenance object. Cross-ref the bug-bounty weakness class: this closes a silent-degradation / trust-laundering hole.

Keep it scoped to math-kernel.mjs references (the only runtime); paths to other files (e.g. fixtures) skip the symbol check. Add a test: a fixture card with implementedBy='...#nonexistentFn' → validation ok:false; a real card stays green. Verify ALL 18 existing cards still pass (their implementedBy symbols are real kernel exports — confirm entropy/klDivergence/etc. all resolve).

# AREA 12: Memory governance overhaul — subconscious dedup + FSRS tune + Track-B/MEMORY.md + rapid-mlx

**Top priority:** MEM-01 — break the dead bootstrap loop. demote=0 is not primarily 'rapid-mlx offline' (the demote pass is model-independent); it is a closed cycle: empty cold store -> no recall hits -> no use-ledger -> mtime-prior scoring -> demote=0 -> empty cold store. Until that is broken, every other item produces no observable effect.

## Improvements

Catalog Card 14 (math-theory-transfer-catalog-2026-06-03.md:183-187) is the ready-to-wire fix: add a SECOND orthogonal demotion axis marginalBits(item, restOfActiveStore) ~= len(gzip(rest+m)) - len(gzip(rest)) normalized by len(gzip(m)). Demote iff (R<rFloor AND marginalBits<redundancyFloor) — keep if EITHER retrievable OR irreducible-given-the-rest. Card 14 is fenced in the catalog ONLY behind 'wants a gzip/Jaccard primitive wired' (catalog line 487) — this builds that primitive.
- **agent scope:** Create _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-mdl.mjs exporting marginalBits(bodyText, restConcatenatedText) using node:zlib gzipSync (pure, embedding-free, no I/O — house style of yuri-fsrs). In planRelocations (memory-relocator.mjs:98), after evaluateRetention, compute the redundancy term for each demote-eligible item against the concatenation of all KEPT item bodies; only confirm a demote when BOTH R<rFloor AND normalizedMarginalBits<redundancyFloor (new knob, default ~0.15, sourced from the fsrs block so it is tunable via energy-weights.json). Surface the redundancy score in the reason string. Add a redundancyFloor entry to the energy-config fsrs validator (yuri-energy-config.mjs:86-94 pattern). Write yuri-mdl.test.mjs proving: identical bodies -> ~0 marginal bits; orthogonal unique body -> high marginal bits; near-duplicate restatement -> low marginal bits.

Integration: a deliberately-duplicated session-resume should demote on the redundancy axis even when R is borderline; a stale-but-unique reference should be KEPT despite low R. Run `node _SYSTEM/Scripts/memory-relocator.mjs` dry-run and confirm reason strings include the redundancy term.

### MEM-04 — Cap MEMORY.md index-hook length to end the 26KB>24.4KB overflow at the source
`harden · effort S · risk low · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** read-lower-bound-not-point  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/claude-memory-write.mjs:221-242
- **why now:** Live, visible defect every session start; one-line fix with immediate operator-facing payoff; independent of all other items.
- **what:** MEMORY.md is 26253 bytes vs the 24986-byte loader limit, so the index only partially loads (system-reminder warning confirms). Root cause: updateIndex() at claude-memory-write.mjs:231 sets `const hook = description || '(no description)'` with NO length cap, and 29 description fields exceed 160 chars (worst: 453 and 387 chars). Fix: truncate the hook to a bounded length in the index generator (the FULL description stays in the source file's frontmatter; only the INDEX line is capped). This is the narrowest correct fix — it does not require editing 130 files, and it self-heals on the next reindex.
- **agent scope:** In updateIndex (claude-memory-write.mjs:231), bound the hook: `const hook = (description || '(no description)').replace(/\s+/g,' ').slice(0, 120).trim()` and append an ellipsis if truncated. Recompute the resulting MEMORY.md size; with ~128 entries at <=~160 chars/line the file lands well under 24.4KB. Run `node _SYSTEM/Scripts/claude-memory-write.mjs reindex` (cmdReindex at line 245) to regenerate. Add a guard: after writing, if the file still exceeds a SIZE_LIMIT constant (set to 24500 to leave headroom), log a warning naming the N longest remaining lines so the operator can shorten descriptions at the source. Do not silently drop entries.
- **verify:** After reindex: `wc -c ~/.claude/projects/-Users-marcelspatz-YURI-OS-MUSUBI/memory/MEMORY.md` < 24986; the system-reminder overflow warning disappears on next session; every one of the 128 entries still present (count lines starting with '- ['); IDE-clickable links preserved.

## Drift findings (8)
- DEAD BOOTSTRAP LOOP (root cause of demote=0): recall() at _SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-recall.mjs:69 only records a use-event (recordUse) for slugs returned by queryCold against the COLD store, which is empty (memory-cold-store.mjs count = cold:0). No cold items -> no recall hits -> no use-events -> ledger file _SYSTEM/state/memory-usage.jsonl is ABSENT -> FSRS scores every item on the mtime prior (memory-relocator.mjs:93) -> nothing crosses rFloor at present time -> demote=0 -> cold store stays empty. The forgetting loop cannot bootstrap itself.
- LEDGER ABSENT: _SYSTEM/state/memory-usage.jsonl does not exist (verified ls: No such file or directory). The 'real USE signal' the entire FSRS design is built on (memory-usage.mjs header, memory-relocator.mjs:12) has never been written. The testing-effect / spacing-effect half of the subconscious is inert.
- rapid-mlx OFFLINE: curl http://localhost:8000/v1/models returns empty; /tmp/kagami-memory-consolidator.log shows 'rapid-mlx not available — skip' on 4 consecutive scheduled runs (2026-06-03 10:02, 16:19; 2026-06-04 04:00, 10:00). The LLM stale/duplicate/contradiction detector (kagami-memory-consolidator.mjs:67-107) never executes. No restart hook and no fallback dedup exists.
- MEMORY.md OVERFLOW: file is 26253 bytes vs the 24986-byte (24.4KB) loader limit — confirmed by the system-reminder warning AND wc -c. Only part loads. Root cause: updateIndex() at claude-memory-write.mjs:231 emits the full `description:` frontmatter verbatim as the index hook with NO length cap; 29 description fields exceed 160 chars, the worst being upgrade-propagation-engine.md at 453 chars and cross-reference-engine.md at 387 chars.
- DEMOTE=0 IS PARTLY BY DESIGN, not only rapid-mlx down: 88 of 129 Track-B files are forceKeep (PROTECTED_TYPES={feedback,user} at memory-relocator.mjs:39 + 87 feedback + 1 user parsed). The orchestrator session brief framed demote=0 as 'rapid-mlx offline', but the subconscious demote pass is INDEPENDENT of rapid-mlx (kagami-memory-consolidator.mjs:198-206 runs it before the MLX check). The real blockers are the dead bootstrap loop + the protected-type majority + the recent-mtime prior, NOT the local model.
- REDUNDANT DOUBLE-FIRE: com.yuri.kagami-memory-consolidator.plist (06:00) and com.yuri.kagami-stale-memory-scan.plist (12:00, MEMORY_STALE_DAYS=14) both ProgramArguments-invoke the SAME script kagami-memory-consolidator.mjs. Two LaunchAgents, one organ, two daily runs with different STALE_DAYS — the 'stale-memory-scan' name is a MISNOMER (it is the consolidator again, not a distinct scanner).
- SALIENCE ALWAYS 0: no memory file carries a `salience:` frontmatter field (grep returned zero). effectiveStability (yuri-fsrs.mjs:55-63) therefore gets salience=0 for every item, so the salience-boost term (weight 1.0) is dead weight — stability is driven only by tier + log1p(useCount), and useCount is also 0 everywhere. Two of the three stability inputs are starved.
- FSRS frontmatter parse mismatch (latent, not currently breaking): files store tier/type NESTED under `metadata:` (e.g. feedback-identity.md), but the relocator regexes parseFrontmatter (memory-relocator.mjs:56-58) use `^\s*type:` which still matches the indented line — so it works today by luck of leniency. A future flat-vs-nested key collision (e.g. a top-level `type:` in body) would mis-parse; the parser is not anchored to the metadata block.

# AREA 13: Cross-cutting seam: Offload organ → Cross-Reference Engine repurpose + Skills-and-Orchestration sector rename (delicate staged migration)

**Top priority:** XREF-00 (the importer-census + slot-split correction). The whole migration rests on one corrected fact: the roadmap's '~16 importers of offload-contract' is shorthand that conflates THREE distinct coupling surfaces with very different blast radii, and — decisively — `offload-contract.mjs` does NOT contain the dead dispatch machinery the plan thinks it's deleting. Live evidence: `offload-contract.mjs` has exactly ONE real ESM importer (`worker-tmux.mjs:10 import { healthProbe }`), ~4 LIVE `route-plan` CLI spawn-consumers (`pulse-orchestrator.mjs:99`, `pulse-lane-dispatch.mjs:39`, `pulse-classify-stdin.mjs:8`, `yuri-lifecycle-controller.mjs:68`), and ~6 manifest/registry `required:true` entries. The token-ledger/queue/lane-bandit machinery that cards 5/7 target lives in SEPARATE files (`token-ledger.mjs`, `offload-queue.mjs`, `offload-runner.mjs`) — grep for `token_ledger|offload_queue|MAX_LANES` in `offload-contract.mjs` returns ZERO. So `buildRoutePlan`/`selectSteeringLane` (offload-contract.mjs:717,1342) is the LIVE pulse-cortex classifier (emits `complexityTier`, `ensemble`, `codexPolicy`, `codexDispatch` consumed by pulse-orchestrator) — it is NOT 'outdated offload guts' and must NOT be deleted; it must be preserved or its 4 consumers migrated to a renamed home. Getting this split wrong = ripping out the post-plan-dispatch-gate routing brain. Everything else stages off this census.

# AREA 14: Cross-Cutting Seam: T3 MUSUBI ONE packaging + OpenClaw write-path portability + CAPSTONE red-team on the YURI code

**Top priority:** PORT-01 (the single central path-resolver). It is the keystone for the entire OpenClaw write-path portability requirement (the explicit moat acceptance criterion: a fresh install writes ZERO files outside the user's configured workspace). Right now portability is half-wired and inconsistent — YURI_STATE_DIR is honored by energy-trace (yuri-energy-trace.mjs:163) and the enforce hook (energy-enforce.mjs:33) but NOT by memory-kernel.mjs:15, and kagami-memory-consolidator.mjs:23 has Marcel's literal machine-id slug `-Users-marcelspatz-YURI-OS-MUSUBI` baked into the SOURCE — that one line ships Marcel's home path to every user. Until ONE resolver owns these paths, every other T3 deliverable (naked-repo boundary, install-time config) is built on sand, and the CAPSTONE will keep finding new hardcoded leaks. It is also low-risk (the yuri-user.mjs single-resolver pattern is already proven in-repo) and unblocks PORT-02/03 + PKG-02.

## Improvements

### PORT-01 — Central path-resolver organ (yuri-paths.mjs) — single source of truth for every write surface
`new-mechanism · effort M · risk low · auto` · deps: 

- **patterns:** replace-hand-tuned-constant  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-paths.mjs (new) · _SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-paths.test.mjs (new) · _SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-user.mjs (template reference, do not modify)
- **why now:** Keystone for all T3 portability; the moat acceptance criterion (zero writes outside configured workspace) is unreachable without it. Half-wired today (YURI_STATE_DIR honored in 2 of ~6 surfaces). Low risk, proven in-repo pattern.
- **what:** Build ONE resolver module `_SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-paths.mjs` modeled exactly on the proven yuri-user.mjs pattern (single resolver, env override, injectable reader, deterministic fallback). It exposes pure functions: `repoRoot()`, `stateDir()` (YURI_STATE_DIR || repoRoot/_SYSTEM/state), `claudeMemoryDir()` (YURI_MEMORY_DIR || derive the project slug from cwd, NEVER a literal machine-id), `kernelDbDir()` (YURI_KERNEL_DIR || repoRoot/_SYSTEM/OS_KERNEL), `selfDir()` (energy-weights home), `auditLog()` (YURI_AUDIT_LOG || homedir/.yuri-audit.log), `configHome()` (YURI_CONFIG_HOME || ~/.config/yuri). Each reads an optional `yuri.config.json` at repo root (the install-time config OpenClaw populates) with env vars overriding file overriding deterministic default. The resolver is the ONLY place that derives repo-root, fixing the 2-vs-3-`..` inconsistency. NO file writes in the resolver itself — pure path computation. Add yuri-paths.test.mjs covering: env override wins, config-file fallback, deterministic default, junk-config fail-safe (falls back, never throws), and a canary that the returned claudeMemoryDir contains NO literal `-Users-marcelspatz-` substring.
- **agent scope:** Create _SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-paths.mjs exporting the path functions above, each: env var -> yuri.config.json field -> deterministic default, never throwing. Derive repoRoot ONCE via fileURLToPath(import.meta.url) and resolve up to the repo root; all other dirs compose off repoRoot(). Mirror yuri-user.mjs's injectable-reader shape for the config file so tests can inject. Write the test file with the 5 cases listed, including the no-machine-id canary. Run `node --test _SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-paths.test.mjs` green. Do NOT migrate any caller in this item (that is PORT-02/03) — ship the resolver + tests standalone so it is reviewable in isolation.
- **verify:** node --test _SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-paths.test.mjs passes; manual: `YURI_STATE_DIR=/tmp/x node -e "import('./_SYSTEM/Scripts/yuri-paths.mjs').then(m=>console.log(m.stateDir()))"` prints /tmp/x; with env unset prints <repo>/_SYSTEM/state; grep the module output for `-Users-marcelspatz` returns nothing.

`harden · effort L · risk med · OWNER-GATED` · deps: PORT-01

### CAP-01 — CAPSTONE: energy-session snapshot write-race — atomic write + lock (CRITICAL invariant)
`harden · effort M · risk med · OWNER-GATED` · deps: 

- **patterns:** gate-on-identity-not-aggregate  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** .claude/hooks/energy-tick.mjs:77 · .claude/hooks/energy-enforce.mjs:82,94 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/energy-breaker.mjs · _SYSTEM/Scripts/atomic-write.mjs (new shared helper) · _SYSTEM/Scripts/branch-lock.mjs (transfer source)
- **why now:** Enforce is ARMED on Marcel's machine (energy-enforce.enabled flag live per moat memory). A torn-write that silently resets a tripped breaker is a real safety-bypass under the parallel dispatch Marcel explicitly favors. The breaker is the layer-2 conscience — a race that resets it defeats its purpose.
- **what:** The breaker is the enforcing safety organ, and its persisted state is subject to a read-modify-write race. Both the PDP (energy-tick.mjs:77, PostToolUse) and the PEP (energy-enforce.mjs:94, PreToolUse) do `fs.readFileSync` then `fs.writeFileSync` on the SAME `_SYSTEM/state/energy-session/<sessionId>.json` with NO atomicity and NO lock. Under Marcel's parallel/batch tool dispatch, two hooks racing the same sessionId can: (a) torn-read a half-written snapshot -> normBreaker fail-opens a legitimately-OPEN breaker back to CLOSED (silent safety reset), or (b) lost-update where the PEP's advanced breaker is clobbered by the PDP's stale write. Fix: a shared atomic-write helper (temp-file + fsync + renameSync — rename is atomic on POSIX) used by BOTH hooks, plus an advisory lockfile (O_EXCL) around the read-modify-write critical section with a bounded stale-lock timeout that fail-OPENS (never block the session on a stuck lock). The repo already has atomic-write precedent (branch-lock.mjs, kagami-event-bus.mjs, task-queue.mjs use renameSync-tmp) — transfer that mechanism to the energy snapshot.
- **agent scope:** Extract a shared `atomicWriteJson(path, obj)` helper (write to path+'.tmp.'+pid, fsync, renameSync over target) from the existing branch-lock/task-queue precedent. Add an advisory lock around the snapshot read-modify-write in BOTH energy-tick.mjs and energy-enforce.mjs: acquire via O_EXCL on a .lock, bounded retry, and on timeout fail-OPEN (proceed without the lock, log to audit) — NEVER block the session. Replace both raw fs.writeFileSync(snapPath,...) calls with atomicWriteJson. Write a concurrency test: spawn N parallel writers on one sessionId, assert the final snapshot is valid JSON every time (no torn reads) and breaker monotonicity holds (an OPEN set by one writer is never silently lost by a concurrent stale write). Owner-gated: modifies the enforcing safety hooks.
- **verify:** New concurrency test spawns ~50 parallel snapshot writers and asserts: every intermediate read parses as valid JSON, and a breaker set OPEN is never observed reverting to CLOSED except via the legitimate cooldown transition; energy-tick.test.mjs + energy-breaker.test.mjs stay green (23/23, 21/21).

### CAP-03 — CAPSTONE prereq: lock the guard-weight barrier terms as unforgeable (freeze-and-canary)
`harden · effort M · risk med · OWNER-GATED` · deps: 

- **patterns:** gate-on-identity-not-aggregate, replace-hand-tuned-constant  ·  **transfers:** 
- **files:** _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-energy-config.mjs:23 · _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-energy.mjs · _SYSTEM/SELF/energy-weights.json · _SYSTEM/Scripts/math/yuri-energy-config.test.mjs
- **why now:** The breaker only matters if its trip-condition weights can't be tampered. This is the single most consequential energy-core invariant and a clean standalone prereq that de-risks CAP-02's LANE-A. Roadmap §9 lists the freeze as non-negotiable but I found no evidence it's enforced in code yet.
- **what:** The roadmap (§9) and moat both demand the guard weights {eta=100 protected-path, theta=10 ladder-floor, lambda=50 fail-closed, kappa=5} be FROZEN barrier terms, never tradeable. Today energy-weights.json (yuri-energy-config.mjs:23) is an override surface — verify whether a crafted energy-weights.json can lower/zero/sign-flip a barrier term and thereby launder a catastrophic verdict (LANE-A of CAP-02, but a standalone prereq because it gates the whole enforcing core's integrity). If the barrier terms ARE overridable, add a freeze: the four barrier weights are read from a frozen constant in code, NOT from the JSON override (the JSON may tune soft terms only), with a startup canary that asserts the live barrier values equal the frozen set and refuses to enforce if they drift. This closes the 'tamper the weights file -> defeat the breaker' attack before the broader red-team.

If the hole exists: split weight loading so barrier terms come from a frozen in-code constant (Object.freeze) and only soft/tunable terms read from JSON; add a startup canary asserting live barrier == frozen. Owner-gated: touches the enforcing energy core's integrity model.
- **verify:** Attack test: an energy-weights.json with eta=0 (or sign-flipped) does NOT lower the protected-path veto — the catastrophic verdict still produces a breaker trip; a startup canary fails-closed (refuses enforce) if the live barrier weights drift from the frozen set; existing energy-config tests stay green.
